# The Tree of Auspicious Timing (Muhurta-Kalpadruma) in Common Language

| Lunar Day Half (Karana) | Lunar Day Half (Karana) | Lunar Day Half (Karana) |
|:---|:---|:---|
| Taitila, Naga, Stuppada <note>These are specific divisions of a lunar day used in Vedic astrology</note> | Gara, Vanija, Vishti, Bava, Balava | Kaulava, Taitila, Shakuni |
| Good rains | Sitting/Stable results | Upward-looking results |

Consider the Sun's position in the "Upward" or "Thief" signs during the five-fold calculation <note>Panchang: the five limbs of the calendar</note>. If it rains while the Sun is "sleeping," the clouds will yield no fruit. However, if the Sun is in a "sitting" position during its transit <note>Sankranti: the Sun's entry into a new zodiac sign</note>, then all grains will be in great abundance and well-being for eating. 28.

### Considering the Results of Vehicles and Other Symbols
**In the Gitika Meter:**
Know the symbols as the Lion, Tiger, Boar, Donkey, Serpent, and Buffalo. Recognize the Horse, Dog, Ram, and Cow-hide as the weapons <note>The "vehicles" or "mounts" associated with specific astrological moments</note>. By knowing these "vehicles," one can estimate the fate of Brahmins, clothing, and grain. Their colors are described as white, yellow, green, pale white, red, and black. 29.

There are also dark, variegated, blanket-like, naked, and dense colors. Understand these as the "weapons" by studying the limbs of astrology. These are called the Musket <note>Tupak: an early firearm or matchlock</note>, Mace, Sword, Staff, Bow, and Tomara <note>an iron crowbar or javelin</note>. Also mentioned are the Spear, Noose, Ankus <note>elephant goad</note>, and the Arrow-bearer. 30.

The foods mentioned are boiled rice, milk-rice pudding <note>Payasa</note>, alms-food, and fried confections. Know also the descriptions of milk, curd, variegated grains, and jaggery. 31.

Honey, clarified butter <note>Ghee</note>, and sugar are mentioned, as are the ointments: Musk, Saffron, beautiful Sandalwood, and Earth <note>clay or sacred soil</note>. 32.

Cow-gallstone <note>Gorochana: used in tilaks</note>, red lac, Java-flower, and turmeric are listed. Antimony <note>Surma</note>, Agarwood, and Camphor are also included; now the castes will be described. Gods, spirits, serpents, birds, axes, deer, and the "King of the Twice-born" <note>Moon or high priest</note>. The Poet-King says there are also the Warrior, Merchant, Laborer, and Mixed castes. 33.

The flowers are the Punnaga, Jasmine, Bakula, Ketaki, Arka, Durva grass, and the Lotus. Also the Mallika, Patala, and Jaya flowers—use your intellect to understand these. The stages of women are described as the Child, the Maiden, the Young Girl, the Youthful Woman, and the Mature Woman. Then the Advanced Woman, the Elderly Woman, the Barren Woman, the Bound Woman, and the Faithful Wife with a son. 34.

**In the Doha Meter:**
Gold, silver, copper, and then iron, lead, and zinc. Bronze, stone, wood, and bamboo are also mentioned by the wise.

Whichever object the Sun "touches" or uses during the transit <note>original: "sankramai"</note>, that specific category of object faces destruction or hardship in the coming period. 36. **Narendra Meter.** From the Sun's transit body, count the constellations <term>Nakshatras: lunar mansions used in Vedic astrology</term> starting from your birth star. The first three signify travel, the next six bring great happiness, and the following three signify physical pain. The six after that bring no benefit, while the final ones indicate great gain or loss of wealth. 37. **Dandak Meter.** If the Sun is strong in the chart, one should seek an audience with the King. If the Moon is strong, perform auspicious deeds. On Tuesday <note>Mars</note>, engage in battle; on Wednesday <note>Mercury</note>, study the scriptures; on Thursday <note>Jupiter</note>, perform a marriage to live in happiness. 38. On Friday <note>Venus</note>, undertake travel to be drenched in the essence of pleasure; on Saturday <note>Saturn</note>, perform rites of initiation. Keeping this knowledge in your heart and opening the "chamber of your intellect," share this wisdom with everyone. 38. **Couplet.** Even if the Moon is very strong, if the birth star <note>Tara</note> is weak, then know the result will be inauspicious; so say all the good poets. 39. If the Moon is strong at the time of the Sun's entry into a new sign <note>original: "Sankranti"</note>, then for that entire month, the people will find their days filled with increasing joy. 40. **Now, the consideration of the Intercalary Month <note>original: "Malmasa," an extra month added to the lunar calendar to keep it aligned with the solar seasons</note>.** When the Sun does not transition into a new sign during a lunar month, the wise declare that month to be an "unclean" or intercalary month. 41. When two solar transitions occur within a single lunar month, the community of great poets says that month is "decayed" or lost <note>original: "Kshaya Mas," a rare occurrence where a month is omitted from the calendar</note>. 42.

### Now begins the Chapter on Planetary Transits. **In the Mattagayand Meter:**
When the Sun, Ketu, Saturn, Mars, and Rahu <note>the "cruel" or malefic planets in Vedic astrology</note> enter the third, sixth, tenth, or eleventh houses from the birth sign, they grant the good results that the heart desires. However, if the Sun, Mars, and Saturn are in the first, second, fourth, fifth, seventh, eighth, ninth, or twelfth houses, they cause distress. But if one calculates the "reverse" positions <note>referring to the Vedha or "obstruction" points</note>, the great sages Shripati, Lalla, and Varahamihira say the results can be favorable. 1. **In the Dandak Meter:** The Moon is exceptionally good in the tenth, third, eleventh, first, sixth, and seventh houses. If the Moon is in the fourth, ninth, eighth, fifth, or second houses, the planets <note>original: "khachar," meaning sky-travelers</note> bestow a heavy burden of sorrow. Mercury <note>original: "Himkar-sut," the son of the Moon</note> brings immense happiness and pride in the second, fourth, sixth, eighth, tenth, and eleventh houses. If the planets do not fall in the fifth, third, ninth, first, or eighth houses <note>the obstruction points</note>, then all the essence of happiness is obtained. 2. In the second, eleventh,

# The Language of Muhurtakalpadruma
<page-num>34</page-num>
->Muhurtakalpadruma Commentary<-

<column-break/>

If anyone asks about the calculations for the sixth, eleventh, eighth, tenth, and fourth houses <note>These refer to specific astrological houses (Bhavas) in a birth chart</note>, how are they determined? First, from the fifth house to the eighth, and then to the end; the Lord of Wealth <note>Venus/Ushana</note> resides in the house of treasures. The numbers eight, seven, one, zero, nine, and five <note>referring to numerical codes for planetary positions</note> are known by the wise to be the positions where planets provide unobstructed happiness. 3.

## Couplet
I have previously described the "Left-Hand Obstruction" <term>Vama-Vedha: a specific type of planetary interference where one planet nullifies the effect of another</term> for the first five. In the same way, understand these others as described by the lineage of poets. 4.

One should accept the obstructions for all planets as stated by the poets. It is said there is no prohibition against an obstruction occurring even between a father and a son <note>referring to the Sun and Saturn, who are mythological father and son but astrological enemies</note>. 4.

The poets' lineages speak of "Pure Obstruction" <term>Shuddha-Vedha</term> from one's own zodiac sign. The Left-Hand Obstruction occurs from the planet's sign to the nine stars <note>Nakshatras</term>. 5.

If an inauspicious planet <note>a malefic like Mars or Saturn</note> is affected by a reverse obstruction, it then produces auspicious results and removes many prohibitions. 6.

If the Lord of the Night <note>the Moon</note> is in the wealth-triangle <note>the 2nd, 5th, or 9th houses</note> during the bright fortnight <term>Shukla Paksha: the waxing phase of the moon</term>, or if planets are in the sixth, eighth, or fourth positions, no one considers that an obstruction. 7.

## Metered Verse
If an eclipse of the Moon or Sun occurs in one's birth star <term>Janma Nakshatra</term>, it foretells the death of that individual. From the birth sign: in the first house it causes bodily pain; in the second, loss of wealth; in the third, wealth; in the fourth, physical suffering. 8.

In the fifth, worry regarding children; in the sixth, happiness; in the seventh, distress to the wife; in the eighth, death approaches. In the ninth, loss of honor; in the tenth and eleventh, happiness and gain; and in the twelfth, one hears news of death. 8.

## Couplet
Through chanting <term>Japa</term>, donating gold, and the gift of cows, even the inauspicious becomes auspicious. If the Sun or Moon fall into an inauspicious state, one should not look at them. 9.

## Elephant-Gait Meter
If the Moon comes to the seventh house while joined with malefic planets or placed between malefics, the great poets say it is powerless and cannot grant good results. However, if it is strong and auspicious, it gives good fruits and is pleasing to everyone's mind. 10.

## Couplet
He whose Moon is strong on the first day of the bright fortnight—all good poets say that Moon will be a giver of happiness for the entire fortnight. 11.

If the Moon is inauspicious on the first day of the dark fortnight <term>Krishna Paksha: the waning phase</term>, it still grants the heart's desired fruits for those fifteen days. 12.

<header>The Tree of Auspicious Times: Vernacular Translation</header>

->Dandaka Meter<-
For Venus, wear a diamond <note>original: "kanee"</note> in the East; for the Moon, a pearl in the Southeast; for Mars, wear a red coral <note>original: "prabal"</note> in the South. For Rahu, wear a hessonite <note>original: "meda"</note> in the Southwest; for Saturn, a blue sapphire <note>original: "neeli"</note> in the West; for Ketu, a cat's eye <note>original: "vaidhurya"</note> in the Northwest. For Jupiter, a yellow sapphire <note>original: "pushparaj"</note> is beneficial in the North; for Mercury, wear an emerald <note>original: "vainateya"</note> in the Northeast. If one wears a ruby <note>original: "manik"</note> for the Sun in the center of a ring, even inauspicious planets will grant steady happiness. 13.

->Savaiya Meter<-
If the Sun or the son of the Earth (Mars) are unfavorable, keep coral on the body. For the Moon and Jupiter, wear their gems (pearl and yellow sapphire) set in gold ornaments. For the Moon and Venus, silver is desired; for Saturn, iron is always preferred. Keep lead for Rahu and Ketu; by doing so, the rivers of sorrow and poverty are crossed. 14.

# Now, the Consideration of the Stars (Tara)
## Doha Meter
Count the stars <note>Nakshatras or lunar mansions</note> from the star of one's birth to the star of the current day.

| Northeast: Mercury <br> Emerald <term>Pavi: A synonym for emerald or precious stone</term> | East: <br> Venus: Diamond | Southeast: Moon: Pearl <br> Center: Sun: Ruby |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| North: Jupiter <br> Yellow Sapphire | Center <br> Sun: Ruby | South: Mars <br> Coral |
| Northwest: <br> Ketu: Cat's Eye | West: <br> Saturn: Blue Sapphire | Southwest: <br> Rahu: Hessonite |

Divide the total by nine; the remainder indicates the specific "Star" (Tara) influence. 15.
The nine influences are known as: Birth, Wealth, Adversity, Prosperity, Obstruction, Realization, Destruction, Friend, and Great Friend. 16.
The first, third, fifth, and seventh stars are unfavorable. All the remaining stars are always favorable. 17.

<insert>Donations for Unfavorable Stars</insert>
In the "Destruction" star, donate gold and sesame; in "Adversity," donate jaggery and similar items. In "Obstruction," one should give salt; for the "Birth" star, give vegetables and such. 18.
If "Adversity," "Obstruction," or "Destruction" occur during the first part of the day, they are entirely forbidden for auspicious works, so say the poets.

In the second section, when "Misfortune" <term>Vipada: the 3rd Nakshatra from the birth star</term>, "Obstacle" <term>Pratyari: the 5th Nakshatra</term>, or "Death" <term>Vadha/Michu: the 7th Nakshatra</term> occur, the first, fourth, and third quarters of the constellation are considered low or debased 20. When these three—Misfortune, Obstacle, and Death—occur in the third month, all four quarters of the constellation become auspicious; so say the lords of the poets 21.

**The Twelve States of the Moon**
Take the number of elapsed constellations starting from Ashwini and multiply by sixty 22. Add the elapsed hours <term>ghari: a unit of 24 minutes</term> of the current constellation. Divide this sum by forty-five 23. Then divide the remainder by twelve to find the state without any doubt. The twelve states are: Travel, Destruction, Death, Victory, Laughter, Sexual Pleasure, Sleep, Enjoyment, Fever, Trembling, and Stability. The Moon wanders through these twelve states and gives results corresponding to their names 24-25.

**Medicinal Bath for Planetary Opposition**
<note>Narendra meter</note>
Take Sensitive Plant <note>Mimosa pudica</note>, Costus, Country Mallow, and Nutgrass. Take White Turmeric, Wild Indigo, and Symplocos bark; grind them together. Recite the mantras over these medicines in water and bathe the patient to make them healthy. When the Sun and other planets are unfavorable, this bath will bring favorable results 26.

**The Giving of Commodities**
For the Sun, give a cow; for the Moon, a conch shell; for Mars, give a bull. For Mercury, give gold; for Jupiter, offer yellow garments. For Venus, give a white horse; for Saturn, a black cow. For Rahu <note>the North Node</note>, give a sword; and for Ketu <note>the South Node</note>, give a goat to obtain supreme happiness 27.

**Savaiya Verse**
The Sun stays in the "Five Protections" <note>Pancha-Raksha</note> for five days, Mars for eight, Mercury and Venus for seven. The Moon stays for three hours, Rahu for three months, and Saturn for six months—this is the explanation. For Jupiter, it is said to be one month. This is the rule for obtaining results. This remains for the upcoming zodiac sign; this is known by those who are famous in the world 28.

**On Giving (Charity)**
**Dandak Verse**
If you perform a task during an inauspicious Yoga <note>astrological alignment</note>, give gold. For the Moon, giving a conch shell removes defects. For the Karana <note>half-lunar day</note>, give grain; for the Tithi <note>lunar day</note>, give rice; and for an inauspicious weekday, give gems to fill life with joy. To the Brahmins... <insert>the text breaks off</insert>

### Now begins the Chapter on Life-Cycle Rituals. First, the Observation of First Menstruation.

**Six-line Stanza**
Avoiding the times of <term>Vishti</term> <note>an inauspicious astrological period</note>, <term>Pata</term>, the Sun's entry into a new sign, and eclipses; leaving aside the <term>Rikta</term> <note>Empty days: the 4th, 9th, and 14th of the lunar fortnight</note> days, the sixth, the new moon, and the eighth; and during the months of Magha, Phalguna, Jyeshtha, Shravana, and Ashwin; in the bright fortnight on auspicious days (Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday); when the Soft, Swift, Swati, Fixed, or the three Shravana constellations occur—if a woman’s first menstruation happens then, it brings all auspiciousness. 1.

**Couplet**
If it occurs during illness, at sunset, while sleeping, or during the <term>Vyatipata</term> yoga, it is unlucky. But if she wears white clothes then, a multitude of joys will flourish. 2.

### Now, the Auspicious Time for the Ritual Bath.

**Six-line Stanza**
Hasta, Swati, Ashwini, Dhanishtha, Anuradha, Mrigashira, and the Fixed stars; on an auspicious lunar date and weekday, the ritual bath for the woman after her period should occur. If it ever happens under Mrigashira, Pushya, Swati, Hasta, Ashwini, or Rohini, she will conceive immediately and obtain a heart-stealing child. When the auspicious planets are in the angles (1st, 4th, 7th, 10th) or trines (1st, 5th, 9th), and the malefic planets are in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses—so say the best of kings among pundits after examining all the books. 3.

Avoid the day, the night, the "junction-ends" <note>Gandanta: the transition points between signs/constellations</note>, and times of natural portents. Avoid the two twilights (dawn and dusk) and the days of Mars, Saturn, and the Sun...

### The Results of the First Appearance of Teeth
If teeth appear in the first month, it signifies the destruction of the child. In the second month, it is said to affect the younger brother; in the third, the sister. If in the fourth month, it affects the mother; in the fifth, the elder brother. If teeth appear in the sixth month, one may estimate the gain of all worldly enjoyments. In the seventh month, there is happiness for the father; in the eighth, physical strength; in the ninth and tenth, great mental satisfaction and happiness. If a child's teeth emerge in the eleventh month, know that the gain of happiness for them will be immeasurable. 17.

### Regarding Placing the Child in the Cradle
*Kavitta meter.* When 10, 12, 18, 16, or 10 days have passed <note>The text uses numerical code words: Danta (32/teeth), Bhanu (12/sun), Dhriti (18), Bhupa (16), Bahu (2), Disha (10)</note>, choose the fixed, soft, or light constellations on an auspicious day that pleases the minds of the sages. Know the results by counting from the birth Moon's constellation: groups of five, five, five, five, and seven days. <note>This refers to the Dola Chakra calculation below.</note> The results are estimated as: freedom from disease, death, weakness, or various illnesses, or total happiness. The astrologers, having reflected deeply, declare these outcomes for swinging the child in the cradle. 18.

### Regarding the First Outing
*Doha meter.* The same rising signs, constellations, lunar dates, and weekdays that are prescribed for starting a journey are also declared by the excellent poet Sardar for the child's first outing from the house. 19.

# Muhurta Kalpadruma: Vernacular Commentary

<insert>Now, the First Feeding of Grain (Annaprashana)</insert> <note>Annaprashana is the Hindu rite of passage marking a baby's first intake of solid food.</note>
**Dandaka Meter:** Avoid the 21st lunar mansion <note>original: "Yugamula..." referring to specific astrological combinations</note>. Avoid the Nanda dates <note>the 1st, 6th, and 11th lunar days</note>, the 4th, 9th, 14th, 12th, and the New Moon day. Avoid Saturdays, Tuesdays, and Sundays. Avoid the 8th sign from the birth sign, the 8th house of the ascendant, and the 8th divisional chart (Amsha). Avoid the signs of Pisces, Aries, and Scorpio. For a male child, perform the ceremony in the 6th or 8th month; for a female child, perform it in the 5th or 7th month. Choose fixed, movable, gentle, or light constellations <note>Nakshatras</note>. First, worship the gods, and then perform the first feeding of grain 22. <gloss>Couplet:</gloss> If the benefics are in the 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th, 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, and the 10th house is pure, one attains immeasurable happiness 23. If the 6th or 8th houses are occupied by the Moon, the feeding should not be done. This is what the scholars describe for the feeding ceremony 24. Whatever results are ordained by sages like Garga for the Sun and other planets when placed in the trines, the 12th, or the center houses, are as follows 25: The child may suffer from leprosy, skin diseases, or bile disorders; or they may become a wise person with a long life; or a seeker of pleasure; or suffer from wind-related ailments; or they may never find enough food to fill their stomach 26. If the waning Moon is placed in these positions, then that child will remain a great beggar throughout their life 27.

### Now, the Earth-Entry Ceremony (Bhumi-Praveshanam)
<note>This ceremony involves placing the child on the ground for the first time to observe their inclinations.</note>
**Dandaka Meter:** When the constellations Pushya, Mrigashira, Anuradha, or the light and fixed constellations coincide with an auspicious day, it is favorable. In the 5th month, when Mars is strong, avoid the 4th, 14th, and 9th lunar days. Worship the Earth and the Boar Incarnation <note>Lord Varaha, who lifted the Earth from the ocean</note>, and then conduct the Earth-Entry 28. <gloss>Couplet:</gloss> Place clothes, weapons, pens, and books before the child. Also place gold and silver. Whatever the child grasps with their own hand 29, that object shall determine the child's future profession and livelihood. This is the opinion of the ancients, as told by all the good poets 30.

## Regarding the Piercing of the Ears (Karnavedha)

**Poetic Verse (Kavitta)**
From the start of the ears <note>referring to the ritual timing</note>, take the two Nakshatras <note>lunar mansions</note> of Aditi <note>Punarvasu</note>, the soft and light ones, and the days of Mercury and Jupiter. Avoid the sign of Capricorn. Take these four days of the week. Avoid the months of Pausha and Madhu <note>Chaitra</note>, and the period when Vishnu sleeps <note>Chaturmas</note>. Avoid the birth month, the "empty" days <note>Rikta tithis: 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days</note>, and the evening twilight. Leave aside the sixth, eighth, and seventh months from birth, and trust the sixteenth day for the ceremony. With a pure mind and the auspicious planets in the "angles" <note>Kendra: 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th houses</note> or "trines" <note>Trikona: 1st, 5th, 9th houses</note>, and the cruel planets in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses, perform the ear-piercing. 32.

**Couplet (Doha)**
Take the signs of Taurus, Libra, Sagittarius, or Pisces, provided Venus and Jupiter are in the body <note>the first house/ascendant</note>. Consider the strength of the birth star; so say all the good poets. 33.

## Regarding the Tonsure Ceremony (Chudakarma)

**Poetic Verse (Kavitta)**
The installation of deities or Shiva, marriages, the nine planets, the first haircut <note>Chuda</note>, the coronation of a king, and the sacred thread ceremony have been sung of. These should not be performed during the southern transit of the sun <note>Dakshinayana</note>, when Jupiter or Venus are set <note>combust</note>, or when the child is too young or too old, or during the rising of a comet <note>Ketu</note>. From the birth, take the third or an odd-numbered year; avoid the sixth and eighth years, the time of Vishnu's sleep, and the "empty" days. Do not take the transition days <note>Parvan</note>; perform the shaving on auspicious weekdays. These are the works for kings as described by the poets. 34.

Leaving aside the eighth house from the birth sign or the birth ascendant, all eight planets should be pure <note>well-placed</note>, and Venus must be considered. If the malefic planets are in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses, and Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus are in the "angles" <note>Kendra</note>, then praise that auspicious time. If the waning moon, the Sun, Mars <note>son of the Earth</note>, or Saturn <note>son of the Sun</note> fall in the "angles," the results will follow in order: death, sorrow, lameness, or fever. Immerse your mind in this ocean of wisdom. 35.

**Couplet (Doha)**
The Moon, the light, movable, and soft Nakshatras are auspicious for this work. Therefore, choose them, as is said by the lineages of poets. 36.

If the mother has been pregnant for more than five months, then the shaving of the child's head should not be done; so say all the good poets. 37.

If the child is more than five years old, then even if the mother is pregnant, the fault <note>Dosha</note> is destroyed <note>it is no longer prohibited</note>. 38.

While the mother is in her period of impurity following childbirth or during menstruation, the tonsure does not take place. Poets forbid the ceremony for the eldest son in the month of Jyeshtha...

# Muhurta Kalpadruma: Vernacular Commentary

->**Verse 36**<-
**Savaiya Meter**
For the ceremony of tonsure <note>Mundan: the ritual first haircut of a child</note>, if the strength of the stars <note>Tarabali: a calculation of lunar strength</note> is not favorable, the world may sing of its destruction. However, if the Moon is positioned in a trine <note>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses of a horoscope</note>, or if it reaches the division of a friendly planet, or if it resides in its own division, its exaltation, or the sign of a benefic planet, then it is auspicious. If the Moon is strong in its transit and the flaws of the journeying planets are absent, then all evils are quickly destroyed. 40.

**Doha Meter**
If the master of the house is wicked, he destroys the happiness of the home. But if the master is strong and nourished, then no obstacles come his way. 41.

**Dandak Meter**
One should fear the ninth day, Tuesday, the day of the full moon, the night, and both the morning and evening twilights for the act of shaving <note>Kshaura: ritual shaving or hair cutting</note>. One should also avoid shaving while sitting on a seat, while eating, while wearing ornaments, or immediately after bathing. Those kings who desire a long life should never perform tonsure when departing for war, during the time of a journey, or after applying oil to the body. 42.

**Doha Meter**
At the time of a sacrifice, a wedding, or the birth of a son; or when one is released from captivity; or by the command of a Brahmin or a King—these are always considered appropriate times for shaving, so say the wise. 43.
When bathing in the ocean, visiting a pilgrimage site, or during the mourning period for a deceased relative; or if one's wife is pregnant—one should not perform a shave even by mistake. 44.
It is said that the beard and head should be shaved every five days; following the specific constellations, lunar days, and weekdays for shaving is the duty of kings. 45.
Whatever auspicious ascendant <note>Lagna</note> arises from the shaving constellations, the King of Poets says one should perform the shaving in those specific ascendants. 46.

**Savaiya Meter**
The five stars beginning with Magha, the three stars beginning with Jyeshtha, and the star Krittika, along with Tuesdays and Sundays, should be avoided. Those who have studied astrology say that within one year, one should avoid these "streaks" of bad timing. If one counts Rohini, Vasu <note>Dhanishtha</note>, and the four "Uttara" stars—even if one were equal to the Creator Brahma—performing a shave under these stars will lead one to the temple of the City of Death <note>Yama-pur: the realm of the god of death</note>. 47.

# The Tree of Auspicious Timing (Muhurta-Kalpadruma) in the Vernacular

In the <term>Mishrabha</term> <note>a specific category of mixed lunar mansions</note> and the latter part of Shravana, when the letters are written clearly, then the child's intellect becomes pure. 48.

**Now, the Youth and Old Age of Jupiter and Venus**

**<term>Ghanakshari</term>** <note>a poetic meter consisting of 31 or 32 syllables per line</note>

When Venus rises in the East or the West, it is considered a "child" for the first three to ten days. When it remains fifteen or five days before setting, it should be estimated as "old" in both directions. For fifteen days, the Minister of the King of Gods <note>Jupiter</note> is known to be a child or old in both directions <note>rising and setting</note>. Listen to what the scholars say: it is proper to hear this, but do not bring the thought of performing auspicious deeds to your mind during these times. 49.

**<term>Doha</term>** <note>a rhyming couplet</note>

Leaving aside the directions, some say ten days, others say seven or three days <note>original: "muni" (7) and "ram" (3)</note> are the periods of childhood and old age for Jupiter and Venus for those who have urgent work. 50.

Always including three and a half days with discernment; some scholars describe this as the period of childhood and old age for the King of the Twice-Born <note>the Moon</note>. 51.

**Now, the Commencement of Education (<term>Vidyarambha</term>)**

**<term>Dandak</term>** <note>a long-metered poetic form</note>

Take <term>Mrigashira</term>, <term>Hasta</term>, <term>Shravana</term>, the three <note>Uttara mansions</note>, <term>Ashwini</term>, <term>Mula</term>, the three <term>Purva</term> mansions, <term>Ashlesha</term> <note>original: "bhujang"</note>, and <term>Pushya</term>. Take the four days of the Sun, Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus. Trust in the second, fifth, and sixth lunar days. From the tenth, take the next three <note>11th, 12th, 13th</note> as auspicious. When these fall in a favorable <term>Ekantaka</term> <note>a specific astrological calculation for planetary positions</note>, then the heart lives in great happiness. Worship Ganesha and the Brahmins, give them donations, and begin your education with a joyful heart. 52.

**<term>Doha</term>**

Take <term>Anuradha</term> <note>original: "mitra"</note>, <term>Revati</term>, and <term>Chitra</term> <note>original: "bahuridhuv"</note>, excluding one <note>the first quarter</note>; these are the <term>Udugana</term> <note>lunar mansions/stars</note> to be taken for the practice of archery and weaponry. 53.

**Now, the Thread Ceremony (<term>Vratabandha</term>)**

There are three times for the <term>Upanayana</term> <note>the sacred thread initiation</note>: the Daily (obligatory), the Desired (for a specific result), and the Secondary (substitute). These are known by those who understand Astrology and the Religious Law Codes. 54.

For the twice-born, the Daily time is the eighth year from conception for a Brahmin <note>original: "ish" (11) and "bhanu" (12) refer to ages for other castes</note>. For the Desired time, the ages are described as five or eight years from conception. 55.

Up to sixteen, twenty-two, or twenty-four years is called the Secondary time by the great souls of the world. 56.

Beyond this time, it is not worthy. The Gayatri mantra should not be given then; so say the lineages of the poets. 57.

**<term>Dandak</term>**

Take the fixed, moving, soft, and light lunar mansions, and <term>Mula</term>...

# Muhurta Kalpadruma: Vernacular Commentary
<page-num>44</page-num>

The three "Purva" constellations <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Ashlesha <note>original: "Bhujanga"</note>, and Ardra <note>original: "Isha"</note> should be sung of as auspicious. On Sundays, Thursdays, Fridays, and Mondays, one should bring the third, fifth, and tenth lunar days into consideration. Perform the **Sacred Thread Ceremony** <term>Vratabandha: the initiation ritual where a young boy receives the sacred thread and begins his Vedic studies</term> up to the fifth day of the dark fortnight; however, in the light fortnight, up to the tenth day is considered best. The poets say that the influence of the stars perishes in the third watch of the day <note>late afternoon</note>; listen now to the auspicious timings for the wedding altar within this period. 58.

## Couplet
From the sixth to the tenth day of both lunar fortnights is called "medium" <note>moderately auspicious</note>. The remaining times are described by the pure-hearted scholars as "low" or inferior. 59.

## Gitika Meter
If Jupiter, Monday, or Venus fall in the eighth house <note>an astrological division of the sky</note>, they are all said to be givers of death. Furthermore, if the Moon or Venus are in the twelfth house, death also looms. When malefic planets enter the first, fifth, or eighth houses of the body <note>the birth chart</note>, they give extraordinary suffering of various kinds, and no remedy will then be effective. 60.

## Couplet
He whose eighth, sixth, and twelfth houses are devoid of benefic planets, and who has conquered the enemies of his existence, shall obtain all happiness. 61. If the full Moon is in its exalted state and falls in the house of the body <note>the first house/Ascendant</note>, it grants all happiness and wealth and turns calamities into dust. 62.

## Gitika Meter
Venus and Jupiter—these two are the protectors of the Brahmin lineage. Furthermore, the Sun and Mars are the great and merciful protectors of the Warrior <note>Kshatriya</note> lineage. Know the Moon and Mercury <note>original: "son of the Moon"</note> to be the protectors of the Shudra class. The son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> is described as protecting the various outcaste groups. 63.

## Couplet
Jupiter, Venus, Mars, and Mercury are called "Governors of the Land" by the scholars. Beginning with the **Rig Veda** <note>the oldest of the four Vedas</note>, these planets are the Lords of the various Vedic Branches <term>Shakhesha: the specific planet that rules over the particular branch of the Veda a family follows</term>. 64.

## Gitika Meter
Know that the Sacred Thread Ceremony is best when performed on the day or in the zodiac sign of the Lord of one's Vedic Branch. When that planet is strong in its own or exalted sign during transit, it destroys all sins. Consider the Sun and Moon to be the most powerful; if Venus, Jupiter, and the Lord of the Branch possess five-fold strength, the young student <term>Batu: a boy undergoing initiation</term> will never be deficient in the scriptures. 65. If one performs the ceremony in the birth month, under the birth star, or on the birth date and ascendant, that boy becomes a King of Scholars and remains filled with eternal joy. For the Brahmin family, this is calculated from the time of conception, and for the Warrior lineage, from the second... <note>The text cuts off mid-sentence, discussing the age at which the ceremony should be performed for different castes.</note>

<summary>This page details the astrological strengths and planetary rulers required for a successful Sacred Thread Ceremony (Upanayana), specifically linking certain planets to the four social classes and the different branches of the Vedas.</summary>
<keywords>Upanayana, Vratabandha, Vedic astrology, Muhurta, planetary rulers, Brahmins, Kshatriyas, Rig Veda, Shakhesha</keywords>

<header>The Tree of Auspicious Times: Vernacular Commentary</header>

66. Jupiter is considered purified <note>astrologically favorable</note> when it is positioned in the birth sign, or in the eleventh, second, fifth, seventh, or ninth houses from the birth sign.

| | Lineage | Wisdom | Planets | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Brahmin | Kshatriya | Vaishya | Shudra | Outcaste |
| Jup. Ven. Sun. Mar. | Moon | Mercury | Saturn | |
| Rigveda | Yajurveda | Samaveda | Atharvaveda | Vedic Branches |
| Jupiter | Venus | Mars | Mercury | Days |

When in the sixth, third, or tenth house from the ascendant, the worship is declared pure. However, if it falls in the fourth, eighth, or twelfth houses, it is said that no worship should be performed. This is the wisdom passed down by the ancient sages regarding the groom and the bride. 67

->Jupiter in Cancer, Sagittarius, or Pisces<-

When Jupiter enters the house of a friend or its own zodiacal division <term>Amsha: a fractional subdivision of a sign</term>, it attains the "Gautami" status <note>a highly auspicious state</note>. Even if it resides in the eighth, fourth, or twelfth houses, it still yields auspicious results in these positions. Conversely, if it enters an enemy's house or its sign of debilitation, it gives results as if it were angry or diminished. 68

If one performs the ceremony during the evening of the dark fortnight, on a Saturday, or at night, or if clouds thunder in the morning, it destroys the intellect of the boy undergoing the <term>Vratabandha: the sacred thread ceremony</term>. Do not mistakenly perform it on the "Galagraha" <note>literally 'choked throat'—inauspicious lunar days for Vedic study</note> dates. Give the prescribed offerings to the four, thirteen, seven, seven, four, and eight sages <note>referring to specific ritual donations</note>. 69

If the body <note>the ascendant</note> falls in the division of the Sun or other planets, know these results: one becomes extremely cruel, dull-witted, and devoted to sin, even if they were a reservoir of intellect. One might become a performer of the six prescribed duties <note>Shatkarma: teaching, studying, sacrificing, etc.</note> or a performer of sacrifices, yet remain a great fool. Having observed the opinions of the scriptures, I have stated this conclusion. 70

If the Moon falls in the division of a saintly planet, the child becomes a scholar. If it falls in its own division, it gives immense sorrow. However, if the Moon is in the Punarvasu or Shravana constellations, it is the essence of all happiness. 71

If the Sun and others fall in the "Kantaka" <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>, know these results: they are described as a protector of the earth <note>king</note>, a servant, a merchant, or one who wields weapons. One may be estimated as a reader of many books, wise, and very wealthy. If one serves the <term>Mlechhas: foreigners or those outside the traditional Vedic fold</term>, listen and accept that such is their fate. 72

**Couplet**

In sequence: Venus, Jupiter, Moon, Sun, Mars, and Saturn. If they fall in a virtuous house, they become respectively: devoid of qualities, cruel, shameless, or clever. 73

# The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Times

<center>The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Times (Muhurttakalpadruma)</center>

**Song (Gitika)** || If the Moon is in the sign of Venus, and Venus is located in a trine <note>The 1st, 5th, or 9th house in a horoscope</note>, and Jupiter—the teacher of the gods—is situated in the first house, then the boy will hold the four Vedas <note>original: "Shruti"</note> in the palm of his hand and become a treasury of sacrifice. However, if the boy is born in the division <note>Navamsha</note> of Saturn, he is said to be shameless. 74 ||

**Savaiya** || Know in your heart: the 2nd of the month of Jyeshtha, the 10th of Ashadha, and the 11th of Pausha. In the month of Magha, consider the 12th day of the bright fortnight and the 13th day <note>original: "Bhuta"</note>. These, along with the 8th day of any fortnight and the day of the Sun's transit <note>Sankranti</note>, are recognized as "non-study" days <term>Anadhyaya: days when Vedic recitation is forbidden</term>. For the sacred thread ceremony <term>Yagnopavita</term> and for beginning studies, the wise declare these times as prohibited. 75 ||

**Couplet (Doha)** || On the 13th day until midnight, on the 4th day until the last watch of the night, and on the 7th day until one and a half watches—these are the variations of the evening defects <note>Pradosha</note>. 76 Even without a meal for the boy, if some natural calamity or omen occurs, this also becomes a "non-study" time; however, it passes away once peace-making rituals <note>Shanti</note> are performed. 77 ||

**Dandak** || From Hasta to the three Purvas <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, Purva Bhadrapada</note>, along with Ashlesha, Magha, Mula, and Ardra—these are sung for the followers of the **Rigveda**. Mrigashira, Hasta, Chitra, Revati, Anuradha, Punarvasu, and the fixed constellations <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada, and Rohini</note> are come for the **Yajurveda**. Ashvini, Dhanishtha, Pushya, Hasta, and the three Uttaras are told by the poets for the **Samaveda**. Revati, Bharani, Ashvini, Anuradha, Hasta, Pushya, Dhanishtha, and Punarvasu are heard for the readers of the **Atharvaveda**. 78 ||

**Verse (Chhanda)** || After the ancestral rites for prosperity <note>Nandimukha Shraddha</note>, no other auspicious time <note>Lagna</note> should be sought. If the boy's mother begins her menstrual cycle, he becomes impure and suffers grief. In such a case, perform peace-making rituals, invite the best of Brahmins, and give much charity. Only then should the tonsure <note>Mundan</note>, marriage, and the sacred thread ceremony <term>Upanayana</term> be performed with a joyful heart. 79 ||

**Thus ends the section on the Sacred Thread Ceremony (Vratabandha).**

**Song (Gitika)** || Take the dates and stars for the sacred thread ceremony, but do not take Tuesday. Furthermore, Mars should not be in its setting phase <note>combust</note>; only then perform the ritual excellently. Until the marriage has taken place, the great sages say all these rules apply. All kings who desire auspiciousness for their sons follow these when binding the ceremonial knife. 80 ||

**Couplet (Doha)** || The constellations mentioned for the tonsure ceremony <term>Chaul</term> are also used for the sixteenth year, when all three castes joyfully perform the graduation ceremony <term>Samavartana: the return home after completing Vedic studies</term>. 81 ||

**Thus ends the Chapter on Life-Cycle Rituals (Sanskaraprakaranam).**

# Now Begins the Chapter on Marriage

->।। Couplet (Doha) ।।<-

From a virtuous young woman come Dharma <note>righteousness</note>, wealth, and desire. Such virtue is secured only when the marriage timing is beautiful and auspicious. 1 Therefore, consider the marriage timing with great care; it is through this that all women attain the strength of devotion to their husbands. 2

->।। Poetic Stanza (Kavitta) ।।<-

First, with great effort, take precious gifts and worship the astrologer <term>Ganaka: a traditional Vedic astrologer</term> before asking about the marriage of the bride and groom with a joyful heart. The astrologer then speaks: if the Moon is in the eleventh, second, third, or fifth house, and Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> gazes upon it with a full aspect, or if the ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth or an event</term> is Taurus, Cancer, or Libra and is occupied by benefics or aspected by them, I say with certainty: the marriage will take place, the mind will be immersed in joy, and great wealth will come while misfortunes are cast away. 3

->।। Song Meter (Gitika) ।।<-

If the Moon is in Taurus and Venus <note>original: "Bhrigu"</note> is also present, and the Moon is strong in every way while aspecting the first house <note>the body</note>, then the groom shall obtain an excellent bride. Furthermore, if they are of equal status, the groom shall find the bride to be a source of constant, beautiful happiness. 4 If the Moon <note>original: "Nishinath," Lord of the Night</note> falls in the sixth or eighth house from the ascendant, or if Mars <note>original: "Dharanitanaya," Son of the Earth</note> is in the seventh house accompanied by malefic planets, or if the Moon is in the first house while Mars is in the twelfth, know that such a bride will become a widow within eight years. 5 If a malefic planet sits in the sixth or fifth house from the ascendant, or if a planet is in its sign of debilitation <note>its weakest position</note>, and its enemy planet comes to aspect it, the poet-king says that bride will suffer the death of her children or become a widow. 6

->।। Couplet (Doha) ।।<-

If the Moon of the dark fortnight <note>the waning phase</note> is in the first house, the crown-jewel of poets says one should not perform the marriage. 7 If the Moon is in the sixth or eighth house and is aspected by malefic planets, the assembly of poets says one should not perform the marriage. 8

->।। Poetic Stanza (Kavitta) ।।<-

First, examine the birth charts of both the girl and the boy to see who is stronger. If a yoga <note>astrological combination</note> for early widowhood appears for the girl, then she should perform the "Subhaga" vow <note>a ritual for marital longevity</note> by offering flowers and invitations, and she should worship the Peepal tree <note>original: "Chaldal"</note>. Or, she should perform the circumambulation <note>original: "Bhanvare," the wedding rounds</note> according to the rites with an idol of Lord Vishnu <note>original: "Hari"</note> or a full water-pot, or with a Peepal tree. <insert>Give...</insert> <note>The text cuts off at "di," the start of the next word.</note>

It also suggests remedial rituals, such as symbolic marriage to Vishnu or a tree, to counteract negative omens.</summary>

<header>The Tree of Auspicious Timing: Vernacular Translation</header>

->The Tree of Auspicious Timing: Vernacular Translation<-

If the bride and groom are known to be long-lived, then no one should harbor any further doubts in their mind regarding astrological flaws. 9. **The Happy Woman Meter:** Where the questioner stands, if a woman comes from the left side carrying a child in her left arm, then consider it a sign. If the child is a girl, a daughter will be born; if it is a boy, know that a son will be born. 10. **Couplet:** If the sounds of a lute <note>original: "Veena"</note>, a drum, or a conch shell are heard at that place, then infer it to be an auspicious sign; so says the crown-jewel of poets. 11. If a donkey, a crow, a dog, or a jackal should cry out there, know it to be a great inauspicious omen; so says the leading poet. 12. **Now, the Auspicious Time for the Groom's Examination:** **The Kingly Meter:** Swati, Shravana, the three Purvas <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Uttara Phalguni, Anuradha, and Krittika—if the marriage constellations are joined with these, then perform the selection. Choose an auspicious lunar day <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> and a favorable day of the week for the selection of the groom. Then, by calculating the moment for the gift of the girl <note>Kanyadan</note>, supreme happiness is attained. 13. The three Purvas, the three Uttaras, Krittika, Rohini, and the two Ashleshas—taking these along with an auspicious lunar day and a favorable weekday, as sung by all poets; take the fruits and clothes to the bride's brother and the groom. Worship the groom and give according to one's means to the Brahmins. 14. **The Song Meter:** When Jupiter is strong for the girl, only then perform the auspicious wedding. It should be done in an even-numbered year of her age; do not take an odd year. When the Sun is strong for the groom and positioned favorably, and when the Moon is strong for both, perform the marriage to attain happiness. 15. When the Sun enters the signs of Aquarius, Capricorn, Scorpio, Aries, or Gemini, perform the marriage after due consideration, and the bride and groom will find happiness. Furthermore, in the month of Ashadha, perform it until the tenth day of the bright fortnight. In the months of Chaitra, Pausha, Kartika, and when the Sun is in Aries, Capricorn, or Scorpio, we may also take these times. 16. Do not mistakenly consider the birth date, birth month, or birth constellation for these rites. However, when it is the first pregnancy <note>original: "dohada"</note> of the daughter or son, these may be set aside. If the pregnancy is different from these <insert>subsequent pregnancies</insert>, then the poets say to perform the rites; then one remains endowed with wealth, sons, and grandsons.

## The Tree of Wishes for Auspicious Timing (Muhurtakalpadruma) — Vernacular Commentary

One shall obtain all happiness and wealth. 17. **Madira Meter:** If there are two months of Jyeshtha <note>an intercalary or "leap" month in the Hindu calendar</note> in a year, then marriage in the middle of Jyeshtha is considered mediocre. If there are three "Jyeshthas" <note>referring to the Tri-Jyeshtha dosha: when the groom, the bride, and the lunar month are all Jyeshtha</note>, then the marriage should not be performed at all. Some say that one should avoid the Sun's transit through the Krittika constellation and then perform the marriage even in a Tri-Jyeshtha period. However, if both the bride and groom are the eldest (Jyeshtha) children of their respective families, marriage is never advised. 18.

After a son's marriage, do not even by mistake perform a daughter's marriage for thirteen months. Do not perform a tonsure (head-shaving) ceremony <term>Mundan: a rite of passage for children</term> and then another tonsure; look to the teachings of the sage Kurma. Do not marry off two sisters together with two brothers, nor a paternal uncle with his nephew. For six months, do not perform any barber-related rituals <note>rituals involving ritual shaving</note>, as the poet-king declares openly to all. 19.

**Chaupai Meter:** If a death occurs within three generations of the family, and Death comes to snatch someone away, keep that month of marriage in mind and perform the peace-giving rituals <term>Shanti: rites to neutralize bad omens</term> after the period of ritual impurity <term>Sutaka: impurity following a birth or death</term> ends. 20.

The saints say it is not proper to perform a tonsure or a thread ceremony <term>Vratabandha: Upanayana or initiation</term> immediately following a marriage. If a thread ceremony was performed during the Kurma-cycle, then a tonsure cannot be performed therein. 21.

**Doha Meter:** If the Hindu New Year changes during the interval, then one may send the bride to her new home <note>the 'Vida' or departure ceremony</note>; in that case, the wise poets say no fault is incurred. 22.

In the Rupamala meter: If the bride and groom are born in the Ashlesha <note>the Serpent</note> constellation, their marriage results in the end of their offspring. 23. If the bride is born in the Mula constellation, she destroys her father-in-law; if in Jyeshtha, she destroys her husband's elder brother. If she is born in Vishakha <note>the 'Two-Deity' star</note>, she destroys her husband's younger brother. 24.

However, if she is born in the first three quarters of the Vishakha constellation, the learned scholars, having reflected, say she will bring happiness to her brother-in-law. 25. The final quarter of Mula and the first quarter of Ashlesha are praised by the wise poets for the birth of daughters and sons. 26.

The Varna (Class), Vashya (Influence), Tara (Star), Yoni (Nature), Graha-Maitri (Planetary Friendship), Gana (Temperament), Bhakuta (Love), and Nadi (Pulse)—the scholars call these the wondrous points of comparison <note>referring to the Ashtakuta or 36-point match-making system</note>. 27.

**Regarding the Varna (Class):** The Warrior, the Merchant, the Laborer, and fourthly, the Priest—consider these the four classes. These are determined by rotating through the zodiac signs starting from Aries, repeating three times. 28. If the groom's class is higher than the bride's class...

<header>The Language of the Muhurta Kalpadruma <note>The "Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Auspicious Timing"</note></header>

...it occurs. All wise poets declare that such a match should be considered forbidden. 26.

### Concerning Vashya <note>Vashya refers to the mutual attraction or "amenability" between signs</note>
**Charchari Meter:** Except for the Lion <note>Leo</note>, the four-legged creatures <note>quadrupeds</note> are under the influence of the Human signs.

| The Varna Cycle <note>Classification by social/spiritual temperament</note> | | | | The Vashya Cycle <note>Classification by physical nature</note> | | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Brahmin | Kshatriya | Vaishya | Shudra | Human | Quadruped | Aquatic | Insect |
| 12, 8, 4 | 1, 5, 9 | 6, 2, 10 | 0, 3, 11 | 6, 7, 3 | 1, 2, 5, 8, 10 | 10, 30, 11, 12 | 8, 4 |

The aquatic creatures are the prey <note>food</note> for those with powerful and large bodies. Although the Scorpion and Snake are also prey, they still cause fear in people. The wise declare that the Human <note>Virgo</note> and the Scales <note>Libra</note> are indicated by the poets as distinct. 30. The Ram, the Bull, the Lion, and the first half of the Deer <note>Capricorn</note>—all these are described as quadrupeds by the crest-jewel of wise poets. 31. The latter half of the Deer, the Water-bearer <note>Aquarius</note>, and the Fish <note>Pisces</note> are called aquatic creatures. The Crab <note>Cancer</note> and the Scorpion <note>Scorpio</note> should be known as insects; so all the saints have explained. 31.

### Concerning Tara <note>The strength of the birth stars</note>
**Chaupai Meter:** Count from the constellation of the bride to that of the groom, and from the groom to the bride. Divide the total by nine; if the remainder is three, five, or seven, the sages say it is inauspicious. 32.

### Concerning Yoni <note>The "Yoni" classification matches couples based on animal archetypes associated with their birth stars</note>

The Lion belongs to the Sun and the Ancient <note>Dhanishta and Purva Bhadrapada</note>. The Elephant belongs to the Nourisher and the Creator <note>Bharani and Revati</note>. The Ram belongs to the Fire-star <note>Krittika and Pushya</note>. The Monkey belongs to the Hearing-star <note>Shravana and Purva Ashadha</note>. The Mongoose belongs to the Victorious and the Universal <note>Abhijit and Uttara Ashadha</note>. The Serpent belongs to the Red-one and the Deer-head <note>Rohini and Mrigashira</note>. The Deer belongs to the Friend and the King of Gods <note>Anuradha and Jyeshtha</note>. The Dog belongs to the Root and the Destroyer <note>Mula and Ardra</note>. 33. **Chaupai Meter:** The Rat pair is found in Magha and...

# The Tree of Desires for Auspicious Timing (Muhurta-Kalpadruma) - Vernacular Commentary

Explain [the qualities]; know the forms of the tiger and the leopard. Ahirbudhnya and others...

->The Chart of Planetary Friendships and Enmities<-

| Sun | Moon | Mars | Merc. | Jup. | Ven. | Sat. | Planet |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Mars, Moon, <br> Jup. | Merc., <br> Ven. | Sun, Moon, <br> Jup. | Sun, <br> Ven. | Sun, Moon, <br> Mars | Sat., <br> Merc. | Ven., <br> Merc. | **Friend** |
| Merc. | Mars, Jup., <br> Ven., Sat. | Merc. | Sat., Jup., <br> Mars | Sat. | Mars, Jup. | Jup. | **Neutral** |
| Ven., <br> Sat. | 0 <br> 0 | Ven., <br> Sat. | Moon | Merc., <br> Ven. | Sun, <br> Moon | Sun, Moon, <br> 0 | **Enemy** |

<note>The table above uses abbreviations for the planets: Sun (Su), Moon (Mo), Mars (Ma), Mercury (Me), Jupiter (Ju), Venus (Ve), and Saturn (Sa). In Vedic astrology, these relationships are fundamental for determining the strength of a planet in a specific horoscope.</note>

...the path of Yama is 34. **Couplet:** All others are always said to be auspicious; however, whoever has an enmity with another, one should abandon that [timing or association] 35.

**Now, the Friendships and Enmities of the Planets.** **Dandaka Meter:** The friends of the <term>Sun</term> (Suraj) are <term>Jupiter</term> (Guru), the <term>Moon</term> (Chandrama), and the Son of the Earth (<term>Mars</term>); his enemies are <term>Venus</term> (Bhrigu) and <term>Saturn</term> (Manda), while <term>Mercury</term> (Somaja) is known as neutral. For the <term>Moon</term>, the Son of the Moon (<term>Mercury</term>) and the Sun are great friends; all others are neutral, and none are called enemies.

For <term>Mars</term>, the friends are the Moon (Himakar), Jupiter, and the Sun (Dinkar); Venus and Saturn are enemies, and Mercury is stated to be neutral. For <term>Mercury</term> (Budh), Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars are neutral; the Sun and Venus are friends, while the Moon (Sudhakar) alone is disliked [as an enemy] 36. For <term>Jupiter</term> (Jiva), the Sun, Mars, and the Moon are great friends; the enemies are Mercury and Venus, and the Son of the Sun (<term>Saturn</term>) is neutral.

For <term>Venus</term> (Ushana), Mercury and Saturn are great friends; Mars and Jupiter are neutral, and the Sun, Moon, and Mars are enemies. Those who understand this deeply and explain it will find their joy doubling day by day 37.

**Now, the Consideration of the Temperamental Groups (Ganas).** **Ghanakshari Meter:** <term>Shravana</term>, <term>Pusha</term> (Revati), <term>Swati</term>, <term>Anuradha</term>, <term>Mrigashira</term>, <term>Kara</term> (Hasta), <term>Haya</term> (Ashwini), and <term>Aditi</term> (Punarvasu)—these pairs are all of the <term>Divine Group</term> (Deva Gana). <term>Rohini</term> and <term>Bharani</term>, and the three stars of Mahesh (<term>Ardra</term> and the <term>Purva</term> and <term>Uttara</term> Phalguni/Ashadha/Bhadrapada sets)—people call these the <term>Human Group</term> (Manushya Gana).

<term>Magha</term>, <term>Ahi</term> (Ashlesha), <term>Mula</term>, <term>Vasu</term> (Dhanishta), <term>Shakra</term> (Jyeshtha), <term>Vishakha</term>, <term>Taksha</term> (Chitra), <term>Anala</term> (Krittika), and <term>Varuna</term> (Shatabhisha) are those who consume raw food and meat; they are the <term>Demonic Group</term> (Rakshasa Gana). In the Age of Kali, the middle [Human] and the Immortal [Divine] are preferred over the Night-Walkers [Demons]...

**Couplet**

### Concerning the Compatibility of Zodiac Signs (Rashikuta)
**Charchari Meter**

->The Chart of Temperaments (Gana-Chakra)<-

| Divine Temperament | Human Temperament | Demonic Temperament |
|-------|----------|----------|
| Shravana, Revati, Swati, Anuradha | Rohini, Bharani, Ardra, the three Purvas | Magha, Ashlesha, Mula, Dhanishta |

If the eighth and eighth positions <note>The "Shadashtaka" or 6-8 relationship between signs</note> occur between the couple, do not marry even by mistake; the poets say it brings death. If the signs fall in the fifth and ninth positions, it results in the loss of progeny. If they fall in the second and twelfth positions, it brings wealth, and other positions are also considered favorable; thus the poets speak the truth. 40.

**Dandaka Meter**
If the <term>Bhakuta</term><note>The relative distance between the moon signs of the couple.</note> is unfavorable, but the lords of the signs are the same or are mutual friends, and if there is purity of the <term>Nadi</term><note>The pulse or physiological constitution based on stars.</note>, then do not consider it a fault at all. The wise poets say one may proceed without any doubt if the heart desires. Even if the signs are in a 2-8 relationship, if the lords of the houses are friends or are powerful, the marriage should be pursued. If there is purity of the <term>Tara</term><note>The strength of the birth star.</note> and the signs are under control, then perform the wedding and sing sweet songs. 41.

**Savaiya Meter**
If the lords of the signs are mutual friends, they forcibly destroy the faults of the <term>Gana</term>. If the lords of the <term>Navamsha</term><note>The ninth division of a zodiac sign, used for fine-tuned compatibility.</note> are friends, they grant great happiness and wash away deep sorrows. Even if the signs are inimical, if the <term>Bhakuta</term> is auspicious, it removes fear. The learned pundits sing that a wicked <term>Bhakuta</term> is neutralized if the lords of the signs are friends. 42.

### Concerning the Consideration of the Pulse (Nadi)
**Dandaka Meter**
Write the stars starting from Ashwini in a serpentine motion <note>A specific method of charting the 27 stars to determine the Nadi.</note>; therein, consider the compatibility of the husband and wife. If the stars of the couple both fall in the "Initial" (<term>Adi</term>) pulse, the marriage will not result in well-being. If the stars of both fall in the "Middle" (<term>Madhya</term>) pulse, then Yama <note>The God of Death</note> takes them away unexpectedly. If they fall in the "Final" (<term>Antya</term>) pulse...

43 ॥

### Regarding the Clusters of Stars <term>Nakshatrakuta: a method of matching lunar mansions for marriage</term> (In Savaiya Verse) ॥

From the star Revati to the first part of the "Juice" <note>original: "rasa," a code for the number 6</note>, and from Ashlesha to the middle of the twelfth—so the wise court pundits declare. If both stars fall in the first part, the woman will remain happy in her husband's joy. If they fall in the middle, love will exist between the two, but the husband will remain dearer than life itself to the wife. 44 ॥

### Regarding the Eight Categories <term>Ashtavarga: a system of classifying the alphabet into animal groups for compatibility</term> ॥

The groups starting with A, Ka, Cha, Ta, Ta, Pa, Ya, and Sa are assigned to the Eagle, Cat, Lion, Dog, Snake, Mouse, Deer, and Ram, as the king of poets says. 45 ॥

If the zodiac sign <term>Rashi</term> of both the man and woman is the same but their birth stars <term>Nakshatras</term> are different, or if the birth star is the same but the signs are different, then the defects arising from the Pulse Cycle <term>Nadi-chakra</term> or the Temperament <term>Gana</term> are dissolved, and great happiness is attained. If both have the same birth star but were born in different quarters <term>Charanas</term> of that star, it removes any misfortune. The couple will enjoy boundless days of prosperity; all the pundits say this puts an end to sorrowful faces. 46 ॥

### Other Considerations ॥

If a servant's star comes before the King's star, the wise say service will be destroyed. In the same way, if the debtor's star comes before the creditor's, the world knows that wealth will soon be lost. If the woman's star comes before the man's, all estimate that the household will be without a leader. If the city's star comes before the resident's, the poets declare it steals away all happiness. ॥ 47 ॥

### Regarding the Lords of the Zodiac Signs ॥ (In Savaiya Verse) ॥

The Sun <term>Bhanu</term> sits in Leo and the Moon <term>Chand</term> in Cancer <term>Kulir</term> with great joy, known as the sovereign rulers. Mercury <term>Somaj</term>, Venus <term>Shukra</term>, Mars <term>Mahisut</term>, Jupiter <term>Jiv</term>, and Saturn <term>Shanichar</term>—listen, these five also arrived there. The Sun was given the signs in forward order, and the Moon was given them in reverse order to find happiness. Because of this, those five planets each received two signs, while the Sun and Moon increased their love with just one each. ॥ 48 ॥

### Regarding the Six Divisions: The Hour <term>Hora: a half-sign division of 15 degrees</term> ॥ (In Couplet Form) ॥

Within the first fifteen degrees of an odd-numbered zodiac sign, any planet resides in the Hour of the Sun. Beyond that, it belongs to the Moon. 49. For an even-numbered sign, up to fifteen degrees is the Moon’s Hour; if it exceeds that, it becomes the Sun’s Ho... <note>Text cuts off mid-word "Hora"</note>

# The Tree of Desires for Auspicious Moments: Vernacular Commentary

From ten to twenty degrees, the influence moves to the fifth sign from itself. From twenty to thirty degrees, the influence moves to the ninth sign. 51.

### Now, the Navamsha Chart <note>The Navamsha is the 1/9th division of a zodiac sign, crucial in Vedic astrology for determining the true strength of planets and marriage compatibility.</note>

| 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 | 9 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 30 | : | 30 | : | 30 | : | 30 | : | 30 | : | 30 | . |

<margin>The Dwadashamsha <note>The 1/12th division of a sign.</note></margin>

---

| Mars | Saturn | Mercury | Jupiter | Venus | Planets | Trishamsha <note>The 1/30th division, often used to analyze character and misfortunes.</note> |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | 5 | 8 | 7 | 5 | Deg | rees |

Multiply the planet's position by nine and divide by the total degrees. All wise people know that the calculation extends up to thirty degrees (52). **Now, the Saptamsha <note>The 1/7th division, relating to progeny and children.</note>:** If a planet is at four degrees and seventeen minutes in an odd sign, it does not leave its own sign; if it exceeds this, it moves to the second sign (53). With those same degrees and minutes, if the planet is in an even sign, it reveals its connection starting from the seventh sign (54). **Another rule:** At four degrees and seventeen minutes, the planet moves. In this way, those with pure intellect should understand the method (55). **Now, the Navamsha:** For Aries, Taurus, Libra, and Cancer, count three times. Starting from Aries, state them in their proper sequence (56). Three degrees and twenty minutes <note>term: 3°20' is the exact span of one Navamsha</note> is the measure of these nine divisions. The crown jewels among poets say that the knowledgeable will understand this (57). **Now, the Dwadashamshas:** Up to two degrees and thirty minutes, a planet does not leave its own house. If it exceeds this, it meets and shows affection for the second sign (58). If any planet <insert>celestial body</insert> exceeds five degrees, it runs to and remains manifest in the third sign (59). It moves forward by one sign for every additional eight degrees. In this way, the crown jewels of wise poets understand all divisions (60). **Now, the Trishamshas:** Place the numbers for Mars, Saturn, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus in order: five, five, eight, seven, and five...

# The Tree of Desires for Auspicious Timing: Vernacular Commentary
## Page 56

For an odd-numbered zodiac sign <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, etc.</note>, calculate in the direct order without hesitation (61). For an even-numbered sign, the wise say one should know to calculate in the reverse order (62). Furthermore, by following the method of the degree-divisions <note>Amshas</note>, the calculation becomes clear. It reveals itself through the specific zodiac sign occupied by that planet (63). If the planet is in an odd sign, take the odd sign; if the planet is in an even sign, then the mind should focus on the even sign (64).

-> This concludes the <term>Shadvargas</term> <note>The six types of divisional charts used to determine planetary strength</note>. <-

### Concerning the Junction Points (<term>Gandantas</term>)

**Song Meter**
The final two ghatikas <note>A ghatika is 24 minutes; two ghatikas equal 48 minutes</note> of the constellations Jyeshtha <note>original: "Shakra-taraka"</note>, Revati, and Ashlesha <note>original: "Ahi"</note> are the end-points. The first two ghatikas of Ashwini <note>original: "Haya"</note>, Mula, and Magha <note>original: "Pitri"</note> are the true junction points. All scholars say that thirty palas <note>approximately 12 minutes</note> at the end of the signs Scorpio <note>Ali</note>, Cancer <note>Karka</note>, and Pisces <note>Jhasha</note> are dangerous. Similarly, there is a distinct junction point at the beginning of Sagittarius, Aries, and Leo (65).

**Couplet**
At the beginning of a "Nanda" lunar day <note>the 1st, 6th, or 11th days of the lunar fortnight</note> and at the end of a "Purna" lunar day <note>the 5th, 10th, or 15th days</note>, there is one ghatika of junction point; so say the leaders of the poets (66). If a malefic planet is in its seasonal day and is retrograde in the house of wealth, it creates a "scissors" effect <note>Kartari yoga</note> that bestows death; so says the king of poets (67).

**Song Meter**
Whether from the birth moon-sign or from the rising sign <note>Lagna</note>, if the eighth house is the rising sign of the wedding, do not perform the marriage there; this fact is known everywhere. However, if the lords of those signs are the same, or if they are mutual friends, then all faults are erased; so say the fearless saints (68). When Pisces, Cancer, Taurus, Scorpio, or Capricorn fall in the eighth house, they possess mutual friendship and provide a remedy for the fault. By marrying then, that woman becomes a recipient of all happiness in her heart. Along with wealth, sons, and grandsons, she remains devoted to her husband (69).

If the lord of the house of death <note>8th house</note> or the lord of its divisional portion is in the house of the body <note>1st house</note>, or if the lord of the house of loss <note>12th house</note> or its divisional lord is in the twelfth, it is favorable. But the lord of the house of death does not bring happiness, and the lord of the house of loss brings strife. Having looked through all the technical treatises <note>Tantras</note>, the rows of scholars say this (70).

### The Poisonous Hours (<term>Vishaghati</term>)

**Poetic Meter**
For the constellations Punarvasu <note>Aditi</note>, Swati <note>Anila</note>, Magha, and Revati, the poisonous time starts at the 30th ghatika. For Rohini, it is the 40th. For Ashwini, it is the 50th. For Pushya, Purva Phalguni <note>Bhaga</note>, Chitra, and Dhanishta <note>Vasu</note>, it is the 20th. For Ashlesha <note>Ahi</note>, the 32nd. For Anuradha <note>Mitra</note>, the 10th...

# The Tree of Desired Times (Muhurta-Kalpadruma) in the Vernacular

Thirteen; Indra <note>Jyeshtha</note>, Vishakha, Swati, and Mrigashirsha are fourteen; Mahesh <note>Ardra</note> and Rohini are twenty-one; these are the elapsed times. Aryaman <note>Uttara Phalguni</note>, Varuna <note>Shatabhisha</note>, and Aja-Charana <note>Purva Bhadrapada</note> are eight, ten, and six; Mula is eight; these are the elapsed times of the Poison Period <term>Visha-kala: a specific duration within a lunar mansion considered spiritually toxic or inauspicious</term>. || 71 || **Couplet** || The Poison-Ghatis <note>a Ghati is 24 minutes</note> of the stars are measured as four Dandas <note>approx. 96 minutes</note> in duration. The masters have revealed these clearly so that the wise may know them. || 72 || **Now, the 15 Muhurtas of the Day** || **Madira Meter** || Shiva, the Serpent, Mitra, the Ancestors, the Vasus, the Waters, and the Universal Gods are gathered here. The Waters, Abhijit, Rohini, Indra, the Twin Gods, and Mula are made known. Varuna, Uttara Phalguni, and Bhaga—all these have been calculated by the virtuous. These are the fifteen Muhurtas <term>Muhurta: a time unit of 48 minutes</term> of the day as sung by the Lord. || 73 || **The Muhurtas of the Night** || **Couplet** || Shiva, the Serpent of the Deep, the One-Footed Goat, Pushan, the Twin Physicians, and the Lord of Death.

### Now, the Poison-Ghatis of the Lunar Mansions

| Ashwini | Bharani | Krittika | Rohini | Mrigashirsha | Ardra | Punarvasu | Pushya | Ashlesha | Magha | Purva Phalguni | Uttara Phalguni | Hasta | Chitra | 0 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

**Regarding the Lunar Mansions for Marriage (Poetry)**

Starting with Krittika, write the twenty-eight stars <note>Nakshatras</note> there, arranging them with great care. Then, place the nine planets starting with the Sun, positioning each one in its desired place according to the calculation 78.

**Couplet**
Whichever lunar mansion falls on the day of the Full Moon, whether just before or just after, write the Sun in that position 79.

# Muhurta Kalpadruma (Vernacular Translation)

The planetary falls <note>Pata: astronomical latitudinal crossings</note>, Harshana, Shula, Sadhya, Ganda, and Vaidhriti yogas are considered. The calculation for the Akshakhanda <note>a specific mathematical constant used in spherical astronomy</note> is 83.

->॥ Kavitta Verse ॥<-

Why, O wise one, do you write the <term>Riju-rekha</term> <note>Straight line; a specific alignment in astrological charts</note> under the star's sun? Consider the twenty-fold division.
When <term>Chitra</term>, <term>Anuradha</term> <note>original: "Mitra"</note>, <term>Revati</term>, <term>Shravana</term>, <term>Ashlesha</term> <note>original: "Ahi"</note>, and <term>Magha</term> fall on the points, observe where they are positioned. Count from <term>Ashwini</term> to the Ascendant's star; then, after careful consideration, declare the result as it may be. If it falls on the straight line, there is a slight flaw; if it falls on the curved line, the "Pata" <note>inauspicious crossing</note> is proclaimed to have occurred. 84.

->॥ Concerning Conjunctions (Yuti) - Gitika Verse ॥<-

When the Moon is in conjunction with the Sun and other planets, the wise describe the results: it may bring poverty, death, or if auspicious, daily increasing happiness. If a woman is born under such a conjunction, she may have a co-wife or become an ascetic; if a "Pata" conjunction occurs, the woman is destined for the city of Yama <note>Death</note>. 85.

->॥ Concerning Obstructions (Vedha) - Trotaka Verse ॥<-

In whichever line the wedding nakshatra <note>lunar mansion</note> resides, if any planet <note>original: "Khechara" - sky-goer</note> appears in that same line, then a great "Vedha" <note>obstruction/piercing</note> occurs, which is forbidden. All great poets and scholars have known and declared this. 86.

->॥ Doha Verse ॥<-

If a planet occupies the fourth or third quarter <note>Charana</note> of a star, it ruins the first and second quarters of the corresponding star in the alignment. 87.

->॥ Abhijit ॥<-

Take the final quarter of <term>Uttara Ashadha</term> and the first four "Dandas" <note>units of time, approx. 96 minutes</note> of <term>Shravana</term>; that period is called <term>Abhijit</term> <note>The "Victorious" intercalary nakshatra, considered highly auspicious</note>. 88.

->॥ Concerning the Seven-Lath Grid (Saptashalaka Chakra) - Doha Verse ॥<-

Draw seven vertical lines and seven horizontal lines; the illustrious poets call this the <term>Saptashalaka</term> <note>A 7x7 grid used to check for planetary obstructions during marriage</note>. 89. Place all the stars there, beginning with <term>Krittika</term>. Within this, the clear explanation of the "Vedha" is considered. 90. Whether a star is free from cruel planets or joined with them, as long as the Moon transits there, it remains tainted. 91.

->॥ Concerning the Seventh House (Yamitra) ॥<-

If a planet comes to the fourteenth star from the wedding star, it is called <term>Yamitra</term>. If it is a benefic planet, know it to be good; if it is a malefic <note>original: "Paap"</note>, it bestows sorrow. 92.

->॥ Concerning the Five-fold Mercury (Budha-panchaka) - Dandaka Verse ॥<-

Write down the numbers six, three, one, eight, and four <note>original: "rasa-rama-shashi-vasu-veda"</note> representing the days passed since the solar transit; add them together. Divide...

# Muhurta Kalpadruma: Vernacular Commentary

<page-num>60</page-num>
-> Muhurta Kalpadruma: Vernacular Commentary <-

Divide the sum by nine; if the remainder is five, then declare the result. If the remainder is one, there is disease; if two, there is fire and great fear; if three, trouble from the king <note>In this context, 'king' refers to the government or legal authorities</note>. If four, there is fear of thieves; and if five, death may occur—this is what should be read aloud. 63.

**Couplet (Doha)**
When you know the *Panchaka* <note>A period of five days considered inauspicious for certain activities</note> has begun, add all the remaining digits together. Then, divide that total by nine. 64. If the remainder is five, all the wise poets say it is a "thorn" (*Shalya*); as soon as it strikes, it increases the pain in one's heart. 65.

**Poetic Meter (Dandaka)**
During the day, there is fear of thieves, disease, and the king; fire and death are always said to occur during the twilight. On Sunday, there is disease; on Tuesday, thieves and fire; on Saturday, death; and on Wednesday, trouble from the king. One should consider these when performing the thread ceremony (*Upanayana*), lighting the domestic fire, or tending to cows. For service to a king, fear of thieves is known. The astrologers who keep these calculations say that one should even abandon the thought of marriage during *Panchaka*, according to the *Smriti* <note>Traditional legal and social codes</note> scriptures. 66.

**According to Another Opinion**
**Song Meter (Gitika)**
Take the number of the day (*Tithi*) and the rising sign (*Lagna*) and add them together. Keep that number in mind and divide it by nine. If the remainder is eight, two, four, six, or one, then disease, fire, the king, thieves, and total destruction occur respectively. 67.

**Couplet (Doha)**
Know this method as described by the learned scholars. However, in the Southern lands, no one follows this particular calculation. 68.

**Concerning the Single-Spike Obstruction (Ekargala)**
**Song Meter (Gitika)**
The astrological combinations (*Yogas*) known as *Vyaghata*, *Vyatipata*, *Vaidhriti*, *Ganda*, *Atiganda*, *Vajra*, and *Shula*, along with the unbroken *Parigha*—on the day these occur, the *Ekargala* <note>A specific malefic astrological alignment that "spikes" or blocks the benefits of a day</note> is formed. The learned of the Moon dynasty say this happens when the Sun is in an odd-numbered lunar mansion (*Nakshatra*). 69.

**Another Method for the Date-Palm Diagram (Khajur)**
**Poetic Meter (Dandaka)**
Draw thirteen slanted lines and one vertical line; place the lunar mansions and the *Yogas* within them after careful thought. If the sum of the *Lagna* and *Yoga* is an odd number, add one to it and then halve it, refining it with your intelligence. If it is an even number, halve it and then add fourteen (*Manu*). Begin the count from the lunar mansion *Ashvini*. When the Sun and Moon fall on the same line...

Understand them in this way: 1, 4, 6, 10, 11, 15, 18, 19, and 20 <note>These numbers likely refer to specific lunar days or astrological degrees</note> are known as Wind, Cloud, Fire, King, Sugar, Death, Disease, Thunderbolt, and Strife; understand these further as causes of loss.
