### On the Remedies for Flaws

**Rupmal Meter** || The flaws known as *Lata* <note>a specific planetary obstruction</note> are avoided in the regions of Malwa, Kurukshetra, and Vahika. The flaw of *Ekargala* <note>literally 'one bolt,' a specific solar-lunar misalignment</note> is avoided in the regions of Shami. The poets of pure intellect say that where *Vedha* <note>interference</note> exists on all four sides, it is forbidden. Do not perform a marriage in these circumstances, as it grants great distress. 6. Regarding the flaws of *Lata*, *Upagraha*, *Kartari*, and *Ekargala*, do not take them into your heart <note>do not worry</note> if the Sun or the Moon is strong in the ascendant; the flaws are then destroyed just as the night vanishes at the rising of the sun. 7.

### On the Aspects of the Planets

**Ghanakshari Meter** || Saturn, the son of the Sun, looks with a full gaze upon the third and tenth houses, while for others, he is estimated to have only a quarter-strength gaze. Jupiter, the teacher of the gods, looks with a full gaze upon the fifth and ninth houses, while for others, the world knows his gaze is only half-strength. Mars looks with a full gaze upon the fourth and eighth houses, while for others, his gaze is considered to be three-quarters. All planets look with a full gaze upon the seventh house; the king of poets describes this for the affairs of kings. 8.

### On Immediate Considerations

**Rupmal Meter** || If the lord of the house of death <note>the 8th house</note> or the lord of the eighth division <note>Navamsha</note> is in the twelfth house, or if the lord of the house of expenditure <note>the 12th house</note> and the lord of the twelfth division are in the twelfth house with affection; the lord of the house of death does not bring happiness, and the lord of the house of expenditure brings strife. Those who are called mathematicians <note>astrologers</note> in this world speak in this manner. 9.

**Dandak Meter** || If the lord of the ascendant <note>Lagna</note> or the lord of the ascendant's division is in the house of the body <note>1st house</note>, or if both are placed there as described; if such a combination occurs, it bestows all happiness and drives the multitude of calamities away from the temple of the heart. If the lord of the house of passion <note>7th house</note> and the lord of the seventh division are both in the house of passion, the astrologers have seen and spoken thus: then that woman, from the day of her marriage, attains happiness day by day; it is said that her joy becomes incomparable. 10.

# The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Moments

If the lords of the first house <note>the house of the self/body</note> and the ninth house <note>the house of religion and fortune</note> are positioned in each other's houses, or if they gaze upon one another with affection <note>original: "sneha," referring to a friendly astrological aspect</note>, then the saints declare that the husband will experience infinite joy. If the lords of the bride's signs are situated in this manner, it grants great happiness to the woman. 11.

### Consideration of Prohibited Hours during Planetary Transitions

**Couplet:** Two, two, ten, eight, eight, six, sixteen, and sixty <note>These numbers refer to specific Ghatis or units of time (24 minutes each) following the sun's entry into a new sign</note>. The learned scholars forbid these specific times during the transitions <note>Sankranti</note> of the Sun and other planets. 12. While the transition of every planet occurs, let no one consider this false; the transition of the Sun is the most famous and its prohibited period is considered extremely inauspicious. 13.

### On Lame, Blind, and Other "Defective" Ascendants

**Rupmal Meter:** The learned poets say that Aries, Taurus, and Leo are always "blind" during the day. Gemini, Cancer, and Virgo are said to be "blind" during the night. Libra and Scorpio are considered "deaf" during the day, while Capricorn and Sagittarius are "deaf" during the night. Aquarius and Pisces are "deaf" during the night; thus speaks the King of Astrology. 14.

### The Results of Planets Joined with the Moon

**Quatrain:** Poverty, death, and the destruction of progeny, along with misfortune and living with a co-wife; these are the results if the Sun and other planets are joined with the Moon, leading to a life of renunciation <note>original: "pravrajya"</note>, as stated by Garga and other sages. 15. However, if Mercury, Jupiter, and the Moon are in the same house, the wise do not consider this a defect. If the Moon meets with these planets in the first, second, sixth, or eighth house with affection, 16. then that man will travel to foreign lands; so say all the good poets and scholars. If a marriage occurs in a "deaf" ascendant <note>Lagna</note>, it leads to poverty; in a "day-blind" sign, it leads to widowhood. In a "night-blind" sign, the children perish, and in a "lame" sign, wealth is destroyed and one suffers daily. 17.

**Couplet:** If the marriage takes place when the Sun is in Gemini, Aquarius, Sagittarius, or the Navamsha <note>the ninth divisional chart</note> of Pisces, the bride becomes a faithful and devoted wife; so say all the good poets. 18.

**Quatrain:** Poets do not recommend marriage in the final Navamsha <note>the last 3 degrees and 20 minutes of a sign</note>. However, if the ninth Navamsha falls in Taurus, then everyone may marry with great happiness. 19. If a movable Navamsha falls within a movable sign, one does not find happiness in such a marriage. If the Moon is in Libra or Capricorn, the wise do not consider it a defect. 20.

**Vijaya Meter:** If Saturn is in the twelfth house and Mars is in the tenth...

# Muhurta Kalpadruma: Vernacular Commentary

<page-num>64</page-num>
->**The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Timing**<-

If Venus sits in the third house, it creates a favorable "sitting." If the Lord of the Ascendant <note>The planet ruling the first house of the birth chart</note> maintains affection for the body (the first house) at night, then the assembly of wicked planets is dispersed. If the Lord of the Ascendant, Venus, and the Moon are in the sixth house, or if a saintly planet <note>A benefic like Jupiter</note> sits with the Lord of the Ascendant and Venus, it is auspicious. If Mars is in the eighth house and all other planets are in the seventh, the pain of poverty will not come near. 21.

**Dandak Meter**
If the Sun, Ketu, Rahu, or Saturn are in the third, sixth, or eleventh (gain) houses; or if Mars is in the third, sixth, or eleventh house from the Ascendant; or if the Moon comes to sit in the eleventh, second, or third houses—know this to be a mine of happiness that steals away all sorrow. If Saturn is in the twelfth, the Moon in the eighth, and Jupiter in the fourth, then even if the bride and groom are wed, they may face death. If Venus falls in the third, eighth, sixth, or twelfth houses, it creates a web of misery for the couple in this world. 22.

Regarding the factors of the "Scissor" <term>Kartari: an astrological condition where a house or planet is hemmed in by malefic planets on both sides</term>, if those planets are in their sign of debilitation or are combust <note>Too close to the Sun</note>, then do not consider the defect. In this same way, if Venus falls in the sixth house or Mars in the eighth house under these specific conditions, do not let the flaw trouble your mind. If the Moon is in its debilitated sign or debilitated Navamsha <note>A one-ninth divisional chart used for fine detail</note> while in the sixth, twelfth, or eighth houses, only then consider it a flaw. Observe the people and the scriptures, and only then declare the marriage timing (Lagna) after such calculation. 23.

**Couplet**
The year, the half-year, the season, the month, the fortnight, the lunar mansion (Nakshatra), and the lunar day (Tithi)—these can bring anger through their various defects, such as being "burnt" (Dagda), "deaf" (Badhir), or "one-eyed" (Kana). <note>These are technical terms for specific inauspicious days or timings</note> 24.

If Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus are in the Center (Kendra) or Trine (Kona) houses, then—provided the seventh house is avoided—they destroy a multitude of defects in an instant. 25.

Leaving the seventh house aside, if Jupiter is in the Center or the ninth or fifth houses, and if the Sun is high (exalted) or placed in the eleventh house of gain, it is auspicious. 26.

If the Moon sits in its "Vargottama" <note>The best division; when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth divisional chart</note> or in its own home, it quickly destroys even a mountain of great defects. 27.

**Couplet**
If the Lord of the Night (the Moon) is in the eleventh house, it scatters and destroys all remaining flaws in a moment. 28.

**Vijaya Meter**
If Mercury is placed in the Center or Trine houses (excluding the seventh), it removes a hundred defects. If Venus comes to these same places, it destroys hundreds of calculated flaws. If Jupiter is in the Ascendant or the eleventh or Center houses, it removes a hundred thousand flaws; it clears away the collection of defects just as a forest fire consumes a heap of cotton.

# The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Timing
## Page 65

<note>original: "Kojuha-jaravai 29"</note> **Now, the Biswa (Units of Strength)**
*Chaupai Meter*
The Sun holds three and a half parts. Venus and Mercury <note>original: "Somaja," son of the Moon</note> each hold two parts. Jupiter holds three, while the Moon <note>original: "Shashidhara"</note> holds five. Saturn <note>original: "Manda"</note>, Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>, and Rahu <note>original: "Ripushashidhara," the enemy of the Moon</note> each hold one and a half parts. 30.

*Doha Meter*
Know the Sun to be the father-in-law and Venus to be the mother-in-law; the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> is the body. The Moon is the husband and master of the seventh house; the poets speak of this with affection. 31. Whichever among these is strong, from that comes great happiness. All the lineages of poets consider this when deliberating on marriage. 32.

**Now, Consideration of Marriage for Mixed Castes**
*Doha Meter*
Taking the letter <note>the marriage invitation or contract</note> in the dark fortnight of the month, if one marries on a Sunday, Tuesday, or Saturday, they will always be happy. 33.

*Narendra Meter*
The Prajapatya, Brahma, Daiva, and Rishi forms of marriage—these four are said to be the best. One should choose the auspicious time <term>Muhurtta</term> according to the prescribed rites for these marriages. The Asura, Gandharva, and Rakshasa forms <note>these refer to historical categories of marriage ranging from voluntary union to forced capture</note> are considered appropriate at any time. However, the eloquent and virtuous say that by performing such marriages, one does not find peace. 34.

*Doha Meter*
<note>This verse uses numerical code words: Veda (4), Nema (?), Guna (3), Rupa (1), Bhuvi (1), Veda (4), Anga (6), Guna (1)</note> Calculate the qualities from the Sun's constellation <term>Nakshatra</term> to the Moon's constellation with discernment. 35. Having understood the auspicious and inauspicious results, then proceed with the marriage. This consideration is very profound, as stated by the lords of poets. 36.

*Chaupai Meter*
When a few days remain before the wedding, the scholars describe this procedure: Take the marriage constellation, the lunar day, and the day of the week; then take the essence of the Moon. 37. On the day when all these are strong, perform the <term>Kandan Dalan</term> <note>the ritual grinding and pounding of grain for the wedding feast</note>. Summon the masons to build the pavilion; it should be of such beauty that it captivates the sight. 38. Then call the clever painters and have many pictures painted there. Construct a beautiful altar <term>Vedi</term> in the courtyard, on the left side of the house, as favored by the wise. 39. Then make the altar lovely—four cubits square and beautiful. It should be one cubit high, with two and two <note>four</note> pillars. 40. Set it upright and cover it with cloth; weave flowers and bind them on all four sides. If the house be...

# Muhurttakalpadruma (Vernacular Translation)

Page 66

->**The Ritual of the Pearl and Wedding Days**<-

Perform the sowing of the seeds. One should obtain a pearl as bright as the power of the goddess <note>original: "shakti-saman-poti-gaj-moti"; refers to a high-quality 'elephant pearl' believed to bring great fortune</note> 41. Beautiful women should sing auspicious songs; hearing them, the generous heart finds fulfillment. When twenty-seven <note>original: "tri-shat-nand"; 3x9=27</note> days remain until the wedding, one should follow the opinion of the sage Varahamihira 42.

### <term>Doha</term> <note>A traditional couplet form in Hindi poetry</note>

Seven, ten, five, ten, five, seven, seven, five, five, five, five, seven, seven <note>original: "muni-dik-shar-dik-van-muni muni-shar-shar-shar-van muni-muni"; these numbers represent specific days for the pre-wedding oil ceremony</note>. These are the days remaining before the wedding when the oil-rubbing ceremony <note>original: "tel-vidhan"</note> should be performed 43. Know the customs according to the zodiac signs of the groom and the bride. The sages have explained these calculations starting from Aries 44.

### <term>Chaupai</term> <note>A four-line rhyming verse</note>

When the Sun is in Libra, Leo, or Virgo, the wedding pillar <note>original: "khambh"</note> should be established in the Northeast direction. When the Sun enters Scorpio, Sagittarius, or Capricorn, then place the pillar in the Left (North) direction 45. When it is known to be in Pisces, Aries, or Aquarius, then the scholars declare the direction of the Rakshasas (Southwest). If it is in Gemini, Cancer, or Taurus, then the pillar should be built in the Southeast (Agni) corner 46.

### <term>Dandak</term> <note>A long-metered poetic form used for elaborate descriptions</note>

Do not let even a hint of worry enter your mind regarding faults <note>term: "dosha"; astrological flaws or imbalances</note> of the lunar mansion, date, half-day, ascendant, or weekday. The faults related to the new moon, the yoga, or the house of death should be considered minor when the planets are friendly. And as for the other faults mentioned, such as the "thorn of death" <note>original: "mrityu-kantak"</note>, do not harbor even a little doubt in your soul. Great scholars, after much reflection, say that these long lists of faults are not to be feared during the "Dust of the Cows" hour 47.

### <term>Rupmal</term> <note>A specific rhythmic poetic meter</note>

Know the time when the sun begins to set. In the winter season, the "Dust of the Cows" <note>term: "Godhuli"; the auspicious twilight hour when cows return from pasture, kicking up dust, believed to neutralize most astrological flaws</note> is always very long. In the summer, it occurs at half-sunset, and in the rainy season, at full sunset. All the leading scholars describe this as the time of Godhuli 48.

### Other Verses

### <term>Chaupai</term>

When the sun is setting, the Godhuli hour on a Thursday is most auspicious. If one travels in any of the three directions except the East, they shall quickly obtain endless wealth 49. While the sun is still visible, scholars say Saturday is auspicious. At that time, if the ascendant is Capricorn, or if the eighth house is the house of the enemy 50, it destroys the bride immediately, even if it otherwise provides easy wealth and happiness...

Those who perform a marriage after carefully considering and avoiding these will always have enthusiasm and joy. 59.

Those men who perform a marriage avoiding all these defects, live with their wives in possession of wealth and children. 160.

# On the Chapter of the Bride’s Entry (Vadhupravesha)

**Couplets (Doha)**
When the marriage of the lovely woman and her husband has been auspiciously completed, the great poets say the <term>Vadhupravesha</term><note>The formal first entry of the bride into the groom's house.</note> should occur within sixteen days. 1.
Within those sixteen days, the even-numbered days are considered auspicious, while the eight odd-numbered days are generally avoided; however, some lineages of poets suggest the odd days may be considered in certain circumstances. 2.
Excluding the tenth day, the even days are preferred; some wise poets of clear intellect describe the seventh day as also being acceptable. 3.
If one is unable to follow this specific timing, what is the remedy? One should then seek a happy time within the odd-numbered years, months, or days. 4.
If the bride remains in her father’s house for more than two years after the wedding, the learned ones affectionately say that a new <term>Vadhupravesha</term> ceremony must be performed. 5.

### Rupamala Meter

The Fixed, Swift, and Gentle stars <note>Refers to specific categories of Nakshatras or lunar mansions.</note>, along with Mula, Shravana, Swati, and Magha, are recommended by all poets for this occasion. Never mistakenly choose the <term>Rikta</term><note>The "empty" lunar days: 4th, 9th, and 14th.</note> days, nor Sunday, Tuesday, or Wednesday; then the bride shall obtain all happiness alongside her husband. 6.

**Couplet (Doha)**
If the woman goes back to her father’s house immediately after the wedding and then attempts to return to her husband in the month of Jyeshtha <note>The third month of the Hindu calendar, falling in May–June.</note>, it is believed to bring harm to the eldest brother-in-law. 7.

# On the Second Homecoming (Dviragamana)
### Ghanakshari Kavitta Meter

In an odd-numbered year, when the Sun is in Aries and Venus is positioned behind or to the left of the woman, that is the time to go. Use the ascendants of Libra, Taurus, Virgo, Gemini, or Pisces, and ensure the lunar date, day, and planetary yogas are auspicious. Choose the Small, Fixed, Movable, or Gentle stars, and give alms to the poor to create joy. Worship Lord Ganesha and then perform the <term>Dviragamana</term><note>The ceremony of the bride's second journey to her husband's home, often occurring after a period of staying with her parents.</note> while singing holy songs of celebration. 8.

### Rupamala Meter

When Venus is in front or to the right, no young girl, newlywed, or pregnant woman should undertake the journey to her husband. The young girl will face great calamity, the newlywed will become barren, and the pregnant woman will lose her child; these words are spoken as truth. 9. If they live in the same village, or if a calamity occurs, or during a marriage, or during the procession of a deity’s chariot, or if a king rises in anger... <note>The text breaks off mid-sentence.</note>

If a bride is returning to her husband's home, or if anyone is traveling to their own house, and Venus happens to be positioned directly in front or to the right, no fault or ill-omen is considered by anyone. 10. If one is staying in their father's house and the "Flower of the Breast" <note>a poetic reference to the onset of puberty or a specific stage of womanhood</note> occurs there, any astrological fault associated with Venus <note>original: "Bhrigunanda"</note> is entirely erased. For those born in the lineages of the sages Angira, Bhrigu, Vatsa, Kashyapa, and Vashistha, the learned say that no such astrological sin or blemish applies. 11.

->### Thus ends the section on the Second Entry into the Husband's Home.<-
<note>original: "Dvira-agamana"</note>

**Now, the Establishment of the Sacred Fire.**
**In the Mattagayanda Meter.**

When the Moon, Mars <note>original: "Mahisuta"</note>, Venus, and Jupiter are not in their debilitated signs <note>signs where their power is weakest</note>, are not combust <note>too close to the sun</note>, are strong, and are not entering an enemy's house; and when the Moon is in the constellations of Revati, Pushya, Mrigashira, Jyeshtha, the Mixed constellations, the Fixed constellations, or the three Uttaras; and if one avoids the *Rikta* lunar days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered "empty"</note>—then establishing the sacred fire brings great happiness. 12.

**Chaupai Meter**

In the rising sign <term>Lagna|The zodiac sign ascending on the eastern horizon at the time of the ritual</term> or its ninth division <term>Navamsha|A sub-chart used to determine the hidden strength of planets</term>, the poets say one should avoid Cancer, Capricorn, Taurus, and Aquarius. The Moon should not be in the trine houses <note>the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note>, nor should the Sun, Jupiter, or Venus be in those trines. 13. The planets should be in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, or in the angles <note>1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note>; then all these planets become affectionate and kind. If other planets are in the 3rd, 6th, 11th, or 10th houses, and the 8th house is pure <note>empty of planets</note>, all wise men say this is auspicious. 14.

**Doha Meter**

When Mars is in Aries, or the Sun is in the sky <note>the 10th house</note>, or Jupiter is in the 7th or 1st house; and when the Moon and Sun sit comfortably in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses; 15. whoever establishes the sacred fire during such a wise alignment of stars will always obtain wealth, happiness, and the fruits of their religious merit. 16.

**Now, the Coronation of a King.**
**In the Ghanakshari Kavitta Meter.**

When the Sun is in its northern course <note>Uttarayana</note>, and the Moon, Jupiter, and Venus are all seen rising with strength in the sky; when Mars, the Sun, the Lord of the Ascendant, the Lord of the current planetary period, and the Lord of the birth sign are all declared strong; choose the days of the Sun, Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus. Do not perform this during the *Rikta* days, at night, or during an intercalary month <note>an extra month added to the lunar calendar to sync with the solar year</note>. Under the constellations of Jyeshtha, Shravana, the Swift, Fixed, or Gentle stars, let the coronation of the King be proclaimed with joy. 17. When Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Aquarius, or Gemini are rising, and the 3rd or 11th houses are favorable, choose that auspicious moment. Malefic planets should fall in the 6th, 3rd, or 11th houses... <note>text cuts off</note>

## The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Times

One should provide the means for calculating the trines and wealth. <note>original: "Atha Yātrā Prakaraṇam"</note>

#### || Couplet ||
<note>original: "Dohā"</note>

O Astrologer, for the King whose time of birth you know, calculate a beautiful auspicious moment <note>Muhūrta</note> and give him counsel. 1. For the King whose birth time you do not know, O Calculator, enlighten him through a "Question" <note>Prashna - a branch of astrology where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked</note> by purifying the ascendant of that moment. 2. If the birth sign or the birth ascendant appears in the "Question" chart, all poets and scholars say that King will achieve victory. 3. If the lord of the birth sign or birth ascendant comes to reside in the current ascendant, the assembly of good poets still predicts victory for the King. 4. Even if the birth sign or ascendant falls in the house of an enemy or a malefic planet, the wise affectionately say the King will still be victorious. 5. If the birth sign or ascendant of the *enemy* appears in the "Question" chart, specifically in the fourth or seventh house, victory is predicted, bringing happiness. 6. Whoever the lords of the enemy's birth sign and ascendant may be, if they fall in the fourth or seventh house, how can there be anything but defeat for them? 7. If the enemy's sign or ascendant falls in an "Upachaya" house <note>houses of growth: 3, 6, 10, or 11</note>, or in the fourth or seventh, then victory is certain and without doubt. 8.

<note>original: "Ghanākṣarī"</note>

When the King asks the astrologer while standing on pleasant ground, and one sees auspicious omens with the eyes or hears them with the ears; if he asks with a loud voice and great respect, then tell him he may depart and all happiness will follow. If at that time there is a "Movable" ascendant <note>signs like Aries or Cancer that favor movement</note> and a brave warrior sees it, I shall describe the fruit of that...

# The Language of the Tree of Desires for Auspicious Timing.

The learned say do not choose these even by mistake: Ashlesha <note>original: "Bhujaga" (Serpent)</note>, Bharani, and Chitra. Any northern direction associated with these, all scholars describe as being like poison. 25. Leave aside Swati and Magha when Venus is in the front <note>A specific astrological positioning where the planet Venus faces the traveler</note>. Having seen the scriptures, I have written this: leave them be. 27. <gloss>Now, the Five Nectars of Life and Death section</gloss>.
The thirteen constellations <note>Nakshatras</note> that Rahu <note>The shadow planet or North Node</note> has already passed are called "Living" <note>Jiva</note>. Those which he is about to consume are known as "Dead" <note>Mruta</note>. 28. The constellation where Rahu is currently situated is called "Kartari" <note>The Scissor</note>. The fifteenth constellation from that is called "Grasta" <note>Eclipsed</note>. 29. These two are like Death itself; cast them far away. The assembly of wise poets says one should only travel during the "Living" phase. 30. If the Sun <note>Martanda</note> is in the "Dead" phase but the Moon is in the "Living" phase, then undertake the journey; your joy will increase day by day. 31. If the Sun is in the "Living" phase but the Moon is in the "Dead" phase, the King will face defeat even if he has a hundred thousand helpers. 32. If both the Moon <note>Him-kar</note> and the Lord of the Day (Sun) are in the "Living" phase, then one conquers all enemies and takes the earth into their own hands. 33. The "Eclipsed" state is better than the "Dead" state, and the "Scissor" state is always better than the "Eclipsed" state—listen to this with your own ears. 34. <gloss>Now, the consideration of Kula and Akula (Family and Non-Family groups)</gloss>.
<gloss>Chhappai meter</gloss>. Swati, Bharani, Hasta, Ashlesha, Anuradha, the fixed stars <note>Rohini and the three Uttaras</note>, Dhanishta, and Revati; along with Sunday, Monday, Saturday, and Thursday, and the odd-numbered lunar dates—these are "Kula." Mula, Ardra, Shatabhisha, and Abhijit, along with Wednesday, the second, sixth, and tenth lunar dates—consider these "Kulakula" <note>Mixed</note>. Mrigashira, Magha, the three Purvas, Shravana, Pushya, Krittika, Chitra, Jyeshtha, along with Friday and Tuesday, and the fourth and eighth lunar dates—the sages call these "Akula." 35. <gloss>Couplet</gloss>. One who stays home should do so in "Kula," one who goes out should do so in "Akula." The assembly of poets says the "Sandhi" <note>junction</note> is for "Kulakula." 36.
<gloss>Now, Rahu on the Path</gloss>. Dharma (Duty), Artha (Wealth), Kama (Desire), and Moksha (Liberation)—these four are named. Starting from Ashwini, write them in the movement of the serpent to consider the wheel. 37. If the Sun is in a "Dharma" constellation and the Moon is in "Wealth" or "Liberation," then traveling the earth...

->### Language Commentary on the Tree of Auspicious Moments<-

### The Wheel of Travel
**Chaupai Meter:** Starting from the month of Pausha <note>December-January</note> and continuing through the twelve months, I shall clarify the effects of the lunar days <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> starting from the first. The poet declares that through the twelfth lunar day, one may determine the outcomes of traveling toward the East and other directions. (42)

**Doha Meter:** The results that apply to the third, fourth, and fifth lunar days also apply to the thirteenth, fourteenth, and fifteenth <note>the Full or New Moon</note> days respectively. (43) Happiness, distress, great wealth, emptiness <note>zero results</note>, poverty, or loss of money—the poet describes these mixed results based on the opinions of the sacred texts. (44)

Distress followed by very sweet happiness, then the gathering of much wealth; profit, comfort, auspiciousness, and then the free acquisition of riches. (45) Profit upon profit, great comfort, then extreme fear, then profit, then death, then wealth; profit, hardship, the meeting of much wealth, great happiness, and then a sudden calamity. (46) Hardship, comfort, then extreme hardship again, followed by a multitude of joys—know this in your heart. Comfort, profit, the fulfillment of one's own work, and then an estimation of hardship. (47) Emptiness, comfort, death, and extreme hardship—so says the crown jewel of astrologers. By considering these factors, one’s work becomes successful. (48)

### Now, the Consideration of the Total Numerical Value
**Paddhari Meter:** Place the values of the lunar day <term>Tithi</term>, the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term>, and the day of the week <term>Var</term> in one place. Consider them carefully. Divide the total by the numbers seven, eight, and three <note>original: "Naga-Naga-Agni," using numerical code words where Naga=7 or 8 and Agni=3</note>. The remaining numbers provide the fruit of the calculation. (49) If the first remainder is zero, know it brings great sorrow. If the middle remainder is zero, it is described as the destruction of wealth. When the final remainder is zero, then death is seen. If the total remains intact <note>non-zero</note>, then victory is yours. (50)

### Now, the Addala Calculation
Count the number of lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> from the Sun's current mansion to the Moon's mansion. Multiply this by seven <note>original: "Naga"</note> and gather them together. Then divide that total by seven. Praise the results according to the remainder. (51) If the remainders are seven or two <note>original: "Muni-Naina"</note>, then the *Addala* yoga occurs, which causes lingering hardship on the road. (52) When the remainders are six or three, then one wanders the earth in fear. (53) When the remainders are one or four <note>original: "Shashi-Veda"</note>, the poet says the result is certain: physical well-being, safety, and the gain of wealth. No evil person shall question you on your path. (53)

**The Half-Vertical Calculation:** Count the mansions from the Sun to the Moon, then add the lunar day and the day of the week...

This is the secret opinion of the Sages, which should be praised and utilized at the time of departure. 55.

### On the Consideration of Harmful Influences (Ghata-Vichara)

The King (1), the Arrow (5), the Limb (8), the Hand (2), the Flavor (6), and the Horse (7) <note>These are coded numerical values used in traditional Sanskrit-style verse: King=1, Arrow=5, etc.</note>. Multiply by ten, and know the Vedas (4) and the Serpent (8). The Lord Shiva (11) and the Sun (12) are the measures given for the Harmful Moon (Ghata-Chandra) <note>Ghata-Chandra refers to days when the Moon is in a position that is considered harmful for a person based on their birth sign</note>, starting from Aries and the rest of the zodiac group. 56. For all other auspicious works in the world, the wise say there is no fault in these. 57.

<note>The table above identifies specific days, stars (Nakshatras), and signs that are "Ghata" (destructive or harmful) for individuals born under each of the twelve zodiac signs. For example, for someone with an Aries birth sign, Sunday is a "Harmful Weekday."</note>

Know that for Aries, the Nanda lunar day, Sunday, and the Magha star are harmful. For Taurus, the harmful influences are the Purna lunar day and the sign of Taurus itself. 58. For Gemini, the Bhadra lunar day, the Swati star, and the Moon are sorrow-inducing. For Cancer, the Bhadra lunar day, the Anuradha star, and Mercury are the agitators. For Leo, Saturday, the Jaya lunar day, and the Mula or Mrigashira stars are always seeking to strike. For Virgo, the poet says the harmful influences are the Purna lunar day and the Shravana star. 58. For Libra, the Rikta lunar day, the Shatabhisha star, and Jupiter are the killers. For Scorpio, the Nanda lunar day, the Revati star, and Venus cause great calamity. For Sagittarius, the Jaya lunar day, the Bharani star, and Venus are called the killers. For Capricorn, the Rikta lunar day, the Rohini star, and Mars are desired as harmful. Again, for Aquarius, the Sages say the Jaya lunar day, the Ardra star, and Jupiter are harmful. For Pisces, Venus, the Ashlesha star, and the Purna lunar day are the killers...

...thus says the poet Visimha 59. **Now concerning the Yogini** <note>Yogini: A group of eight female deities in Hindu astrology whose position in the sky changes daily, influencing the success of journeys.</note>. **Couplet.** East, South-East...

->Now the Yogini Chart.<-

The poet speaks with affection of the directions: East, South-East, South, South-West, West, North-West, North, and North-East. The Yogini always wanders according to the lunar dates <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> starting from the first day.

| 9 | 11 | 13 | 14 | 15 | 17 | 10 | 30 |
<note>The numbers represent the Tithis (lunar days). For example, on the 1st and 9th days of the lunar month, the Yogini resides in the East.</note>

Thus 60. It is harmful to face the Yogini or have her to one's left; it is auspicious to have her at one's back or to the right. The wise declare this specifically for the time of departure and for battle.

Know that in the bright fortnight <note>Shukla Paksha: the waxing phase of the moon</note>, the direction is reversed. Calculate this "Abode of Time" starting from the Sun. In whichever direction the Abode of Time resides, facing that direction brings comfort and success 62. At night, know this to be the opposite. Having it at one's back or to the right is declared auspicious. One who is strong in this regard during travel or war will gain wealth and cause the enemies to lose theirs 63. **Now concerning the "Iron Bar" (Parigha).** The seven stars starting from Krittika are in the East; the seven starting from Magha are in the South; the seven starting from Anuradha are in the West; and the seven starting from Dhanishtha are in the North 64. **Couplet.** The "Iron Bar of Time" (Parigha Kala) exists from the North-West to the South-East.

->Muhurta Kalpadruma in the Vernacular<-

# The Tree of Desired Times (Muhurta Kalpadruma)

This is the remedy: avoid the "Spear of the Day" <note>original: "Tarbar-ko-shul"; a specific astrological affliction based on the day of the week</note> and travel in an auspicious direction. 68. If a retrograde planet falls in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> of that day, do not undertake a journey; so say the wise and those of beautiful intellect. 69.

### The Spear of the Solstice (Ghanakshari Meter)

When both the Sun and the Moon are in the Northern Solstice <term>Uttarayan: the six-month period when the sun moves north</term>, the King may travel toward the East and the North. When both are in the Southern Solstice <term>Dakshinayan</term>, he cannot go toward the South or the West. If the Sun is in a different solstice, traveling in the direction of that solstice during the day brings joy. If the Moon is in a particular solstice, the poets of pure intellect say one should travel in that direction at night. 70.

### Couplet (Doha)

Know the East and West, and the North and South months; the lords of poets say that traveling in the opposite direction leads to destruction and fear. 71.

### Reflections on Venus (Bhrigu-Vichar)
### Paddharika Meter

A wise King does not travel toward the direction where the Son of Bhrigu <note>Venus (Shukra)</note> resides. No one should mistakenly go toward the direction where he is positioned in the celestial sphere. 72. Furthermore, calculate from the constellation Krittika <note>The Pleiades</note> to find his residence among the four directions. Do not go to that direction, for the experts say Venus will quickly snatch away one's life-breath. 73. When Venus is retrograde, combust <term>Ast: hidden by the sun's glare</term>, or in its sign of debilitation, no King should begin a journey. If one travels stubbornly, breaking this rule, the enemy will surround and seize him in battle. 74.

If Venus is combust but Mercury is at the King's back, and the King then charges at the enemy, he will capture the foe and throw them in prison, just as a hawk captures a quail. 75. If Mercury is directly in front of someone as they walk, they will suffer a swift defeat. This is the reflection for a man's first journey, and it is the essential bridge for a King at the time of war. 76.

### Reflections on the Blind Venus (Andha-Shukra-Vichar)
### Paddhari Meter

From the constellation Revati <note>original: "Pusha"</note> to the first quarter of Krittika, Venus is considered "sightless" or blind; this is beyond dispute. Even if he is directly in front and very angry, he does no harm to Kings. 77. If Venus becomes combust while the King is halfway through his journey, the people say the King should stop and remain where he is.

<header>The Tree of Auspicious Timing: Vernacular Commentary</header>

# The Tree of Auspicious Timing: Vernacular Commentary

When a planet rises or is in retrograde motion, one may begin a journey. Even if Venus is directly in front <note>Facing the direction of travel, usually considered inauspicious</note>, what fault can there be? 78.

## Couplet
If the Sun or a planet is in the sign of Pisces and also in the subdivision <term>Amsha: a one-ninth or one-thirtieth division of a zodiac sign</term> of Pisces at that moment, and is moving in retrograde, then the wise and virtuous should know it is a time for choosing a groom. 79.

If the Lord of the birth sign or the Lord of the ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific time</term> enters the current ascendant, it grants great happiness even on an otherwise inauspicious day; so say all the great poets. 80.

If someone begins a journey when the sign is Aquarius and the subdivision is also Aquarius, the wise poets say that with every step they take, they suffer a loss of wealth. 81.

## Narendra Meter
If the eighth house from the birth sign or birth ascendant is rising, or if the sixth house <note>The house of enemies</note> from one's enemy's sign is rising, and the lords of these houses are present in the current ascendant, then those men who set out on a journey at that time will surely go to the Temple of Death <note>Meaning they will face fatal consequences</note>. 82.

If the ascendant or the Moon is in the "Best Division" <term>Vargottama: when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth divisional chart, increasing its strength</term> when the king begins his march, then his heart's desires are fulfilled, and it proves most auspicious. If the sign is Aquarius or Capricorn and their respective subdivisions are rising in the ascendant, then those kings who travel to foreign lands by boarding a boat shall find great happiness. 83.

When a king marches against an enemy during an ascendant that aligns with the "Directional Gate" <note>The auspicious direction associated with specific signs</note>, he achieves victory, accumulates wealth, and his pure fame spreads across the earth. But those kings who march against an enemy in a "Reverse Ascendant" <note>Pratiloma: an unfavorable or backward-facing alignment</note> are said to face immense fear day by day, remaining soaked in calamity. 84.

## Couplet
If the birth sign of the king is associated with auspicious planets and that same sign becomes the ascendant at the time of departure, it grants manifold joys. 85.

If the eighth sign from the enemy's sign becomes the ascendant for the king's march, then what wonder is there in his victory? <note>It is certain</note>. 86.

If the sign occupied by the Sun at the time of birth becomes the second house from the current ascendant, then the lord of men <note>The King</note> shall be victorious. 87.

The "Royal Yogas" <term>Rajayoga: specific planetary combinations indicating power and success</term> described by poets in the birth chart—if these same combinations appear at the time of departure, they provide a treasury of happiness. 88.

### Now, the Lords of the Directions
The Sun, Venus, Mars, Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon</note>, Saturn, the Moon, Mercury, and Jupiter—these eight are the lords of the directions.

It highlights the importance of Vargottama positions and warns against traveling during unfavorable Aquarius alignments.</summary>

# The Lalati Combination <note>Lalati-Yoga: A specific planetary alignment believed to bring success or influence</note> || Ghanakshari Meter || If the Moon, the enemy of the Sun, and the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> are in the sky <note>the 10th house</note>
# And the Moon resides in the fourth house, the home of the lotus-lover <note>the Sun</note>.
# If Jupiter sits in the second house, bringing joy to the mind. If in the twelfth...

If they reside in the eighth or ninth
houses. This is the Lalati
combination, according to the
tradition of the texts. It creates a
screen <note>protection</note> for all the planets
in their respective directions 61. Now, the Rules for Setting Out <note>Sthana-vidhi</note> ||
Chaupai Meter || If one begins a journey
under the Mrigashira star,

The wise king will dwell securely in his place. If he sets out under Punarvasu with his army

departs under Hasta,

Except for the South at noon—nowhere should one journey.

# The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Times
<note>original: "Muhurttakalpadruma Bhasha"</note>

Whichever house a cruel planet <note>malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</note> occupies, the wise poets say that house will suffer affliction. 98.

### Royal Meter
<note>original: "Narendra Chhand"</note>
If gentle planets are in the trines or angles <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th, 5th, or 9th houses</note> and cruel planets are in the third, sixth, tenth, or eleventh houses, then if one sets out on a journey, they will receive the full desired fruit of their labor. If the Moon falls in the twelfth or the eighth house <note>the house of death</note> at the time of the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the specific moment of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon</term>, it gives various kinds of inauspicious results; so say the assembly of wise poets. 99.

If Saturn is in the tenth house and Venus is in the seventh, these are said to be extremely inauspicious. Furthermore, if the Lord of the Ascendant is in the seventh, eighth, or sixth house, one suffers immense sorrow. A King should only begin his march when he has the strength of the Yoga <note>astrological combinations</note> and the strength of the Brahmin-stars <note>auspicious lunar mansions</note>. 100.

If the Sun is in the third house, the Moon in the tenth, and Saturn and Mars are situated in the sixth house <note>the house of enemies</note>, and a King marches under this combination, he attains many joys. He will conquer the armies of all his enemies and return home in happiness with great fame. 1.

If Saturn is in the third house and Mars is in the sixth, it is beautiful. If Jupiter is in the body-house <note>the 1st house/Ascendant</note> and Mercury is in the eleventh house <note>the house of gains</note>, and if Venus and Jupiter are favorable, then the King who marches under this yoga will subdue hundreds of thousands of enemies and return home with trumpets sounding. 2.

### Couplet
<note>original: "Doha"</note>
If Jupiter is in the Ascendant and the Moon is in the eighth house, the King who marches then will utterly destroy the enemy's army. 3.

If Jupiter is in the Ascendant, Saturn is in the eleventh or second house, and the Sun is in any other house, the crown-jewels of wise poets say one should begin their march. 4.

If the Moon is in the seventh, the Sun is in the Ascendant, Jupiter is in the fourth, and Venus is in the second house, the King who moves at this time will win the battle against his enemies. 5.

### Paddhari Meter
If Venus is in the Ascendant and Mercury is in the house of wealth <note>the 2nd house</note>, and the Sun is affectionately placed in the third, the King who marches then is like a lamp; the enemy "insects" will come and be burnt away entirely. 6.

### Tomar Meter
If the Sun is in the house of the body <note>1st house</note>, Saturn is affectionately in the house of enemies <note>6th house</note>, and the Moon is in the tenth, no one can conquer him. 7.

### Royal Meter
If Mars and Saturn are in the house of the body, the Sun shines in the tenth house, and Mercury sits in the house of gains <note>11th house</note> like Indra <note>King of the Gods</note> providing all comforts; or if Venus is in the tenth and Saturn falls in the eleventh, such a King should begin his march.

# The Tree of Desires for Auspicious Timing
## (Muhurttakalpadruma)

...bringing back the multitude of the enemy's best elephants quickly to his own palace.

### Verse (Doha)
If Saturn, Mars, Venus, Mercury, and Jupiter are strong in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses <note>These are the Upachaya or 'houses of growth' where malefic planets often yield positive results in worldly struggles</note>, and the King sets out for battle, he shall achieve a great victory. (9)

### Verse (Paddhari)
If Jupiter is in the first house <term>Tanugeha: the Ascendant or house of self</term> and Saturn is in the seventh house <term>Madana: the house of partnerships or open enemies</term>; if Venus and the Moon are in the fourth house <term>Sukhabhavana: the house of happiness and home</term>; and if the Sun is in the third house <term>Sahajadhama: the house of prowess</term>, then the King will surely conquer the villages of his enemy's hosts. (10)

If Jupiter and the Moon are in the first house, the Sun is in the seventh, and Mercury and Venus are in the eleventh house <term>Labhamandira: the house of gains</term>; if Saturn and Mars are in the third house, then the King sets out with affection <note>meaning with confidence and ease</note>. (11)

The King who marches out under this yoga <note>astrological combination</note>, sounding the war-drums along with his army, will strike down the enemy's elephant forces just as a lion kills its prey. (12)

If Jupiter or the Moon is in the first house, and the Sun is in the sixth house <term>Ripugraha: the house of enemies</term>; if Mercury is in the fifth house <term>Sutabhana: the house of intellect/children</term>, Saturn is in the tenth, and Venus is happily situated in the house of a friend. (13)

If a strong Mercury is placed in the Ascendant, and Jupiter is seated in a quadrant <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>; and if a waning Moon is placed in the twelfth, third, sixth, or ninth houses. (14)

### Verse (Narendra)
Leaving aside the seventh, eighth, and ninth houses, if the malefic planets <note>Sun, Mars, Saturn, Rahu, Ketu</note> are seated in any other houses; and if they specifically occupy the fourth, third, or eleventh houses; and if Jupiter in a quadrant looks upon Venus—should any King march then, even if his enemy is as powerful as Indra <note>the King of the Gods</note>, that enemy will seek his protection. (15)

### Verse (Paddhari)
If Mercury is in the sixth or fourth house and is viewed by a benefic planet with a friendly gaze; and if the malefic planets are seated with affection in any house except the twelfth, first, or seventh. (16)

Those people who march out under this yoga will burn the enemy's city and lay it to waste. (17)

If Jupiter takes its place in the first house, and the malefic planets are in the eleventh or tenth houses; if any Lord of Men <note>King</note> travels under this yoga, he becomes the ruler of his enemy's country. (18)

If Venus and Mercury are in the seventh house, and the Moon is in the fourth house; if any Lord of Men travels under this yoga...

# The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Times
<note>original: "Muhurtta Kalpadruma Bhasha"</note>

If the king of a country sets out against his own enemy <insert>under these conditions</insert>, he shall be victorious 18.

**Couplet**
If Venus and Jupiter are in the Ascendant <term>Lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the march</term> and the Moon is in the eighth house <term>Nidhan; the house of death or transformation</term>, then any monarch who marches will conquer even a powerful foe 20. If Mercury and Venus are in the fourth house <term>Sukha Geha; the house of happiness</term> and the Moon is in the seventh house <term>Madan Sadan; the house of desire/partnerships</term>, the king who travels under this alignment shall take the enemy into his own hands 21. If the Moon resides in the fourth house while Mercury and Venus are positioned on either side of it, the king should march against his enemy and he will surely conquer 22.

**Paddhati Meter**
If Venus is in the first house, and Mars <term>Bhumi Suta; son of the Earth</term> is affectionately placed in the sixth house <term>the house of enemies</term>, while Mercury is in the fourth and Saturn is in the third house, the King who marches shall conquer the enemy's abode 23.

**Narendra Meter**
If the Sun is in the sixth house, the Moon and Mars are in the tenth house <term>Karam Sadan; the house of action/profession</term>, Mercury and Jupiter are in the Ascendant, Venus is in the eleventh house <term>Mitra Bhavan; the house of gains/friends</term>, and Saturn <term>Ravinandan; son of the Sun</term> is in the eleventh house—if a king marches on a Thursday <note>Jupiter's day</note> under this alignment, he shall instantly strip his enemies of their land and wealth 24.

**Couplet**
If Venus and Mars are in the third house, Mercury is in the seventh, the Sun is in the sixth, and Jupiter is in the Ascendant, the king who marches shall conquer the enemy with a flourish of trumpets 25. If Jupiter, Venus, and the Sun are in the third or fourth houses, and Saturn and Mars are in the sixth, the king who travels in this alignment conquers the multitude of his foes 26.

### Concerning the Yoga, Adhiyoga, and Yogadhiyoga Alignments

**Kavitta Meter**
If one planet among Jupiter, Mercury, or Venus falls in a trine <term>Trikon; 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term> or a quadrant <term>Kantak/Kendra; 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term>, it is called **Yoga**. If two of these planets are so placed, it is **Adhiyoga**. If all three are positioned there, people describe it as **Yogadhiyoga**. Marching during a *Yoga* ensures a safe journey; in an *Adhiyoga*, one returns with victory and great fame. If a king sets out during a *Yogadhiyoga*, he conquers the entire circle of the earth and seizes the lands of enemies who refuse to yield 27.

### Concerning the Alignment of Fortune
<note>original: "Bhagya Yoga"</note>

**Couplet**
If Mercury is in the fifth house, the Sun is in the sixth, and Jupiter is in the ninth, this is called the **Alignment of Fortune**; it causes the destruction of enemies 28.

### Concerning the Alignment of Welfare
<note>original: "Kalyan Yoga"</note>

If Jupiter is in a trine or quadrant, and the Sun is in the eleventh or tenth house, this is called the **Alignment of Welfare**; it quickly grants auspicious results 29.

### The Alignment of Victory
<note>original: "Vijay Yoga"</note>

If the Lord of the Direction <term>Digisha; the planetary ruler of the direction in which the king is traveling</term> is in the Ascendant and is friendly to the heart... Victory <note>The text breaks here; the catchword "Vijay" indicates the next page continues this thought.</note>

-># The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Times<-
<note>original: "Muhurta Kalpadruma Bhasha"</note>

...all poets and scholars call this the 30th Yoga.

### The Chintamani Yoga
<note>Chintamani refers to a mythical wish-fulfilling gem.</note>
When Mars <term>Bhusuta; son of the earth</term> is in the third house and Jupiter occupies the ninth house, the assembly of skilled astrologers calls this the Chintamani Yoga. 31.

### The Lion Yoga
If Venus <term>Bhrigu</term> is in the first house <term>Tan; the body</term>, the Moon <term>Shashi</term> is in the fourth house <term>Sukha-sadana; the house of happiness</term>, and Jupiter <term>Jiva</term> is in the tenth house <term>Karma-dhama; the house of action</term>, it is called the Lion Yoga. It fulfills all the desires of the mind. 32.

### The Victory Over Death Yoga
<note>original: "Mrityunjaya Yoga"</note>
When Mars is in the first house, Jupiter is in the eighth, and the Sun <term>Divakara; the day-maker</term> is also in the eighth, this is called the Victory Over Death Yoga. It destroys one's enemies. 33.

### The Quadrant Yoga
<note>original: "Kendra Yoga"</note>
When the Moon is in the <term>Apoklima</term> <note>the 3rd, 6th, 9th, or 12th houses</note> and Jupiter is in a <term>Kantaka</term> <note>a quadrant or 'thorn' house: 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note>, it is called the Quadrant Yoga. It grants all the heart's desires. 34.

### The Pigeon Yoga
<note>original: "Paravata Yoga"</note>
If the Moon is in the first house and Jupiter is in the seventh house <term>Ketana Mandir</term>, this combination is the Pigeon Yoga, which brings great happiness. 35.

### The Bow Yoga
<note>original: "Pinaka Yoga"</note>
When the Sun is in the eighth house, Venus is in the eighth, and Mars <term>Dharanikonanda; son of the earth</term> is in the third house, it is called the Bow Yoga. It brings increasing joy day by day. 36.

### The Death Yoga
If Venus is in the Sun's sign <note>Leo</note> and the Sun is in Venus's sign <note>Taurus or Libra</note>, or if both are in the same sign, know this to be the Death Yoga. 37.

### The Life Yoga
When the Moon is in its sign of exaltation and Jupiter and the Sun are both strong, know this to be the Life-Giving Yoga <term>Sanjivan</term>, which takes away the fear of death. 38.

### The Terrible Yoga
<note>original: "Bhayanak Yoga"</note>
When planets are situated in the seventh house from each other or in each other's signs, it creates a very fearful combination called the Terrible Yoga. 39.

### The Fearless Yoga
When Venus and Jupiter are in their <term>Moolatrikona</term> <note>specific auspicious degrees of a sign</note> or exaltation signs, it is called the Fearless Yoga. It destroys all great fears. 40.

### The Tearer Yoga
<note>original: "Vidarak Yoga"</note>
If the lord of the seventh house is in the twelfth house in its sign of debilitation, and Saturn is in the fifth house, this "Tearer" Yoga destroys one's wife. 41.

### The Family-Destroyer Yoga
If the Moon is in the seventh house and a malefic planet is also in the seventh house, this is the Family-Destroyer Yoga. It quickly takes away one's wife. 42.

### The Sinful-Bodice Yoga
<note>original: "Papakanchuki Yoga"</note>
If the lord of the house of death is weak and situated between malefic planets, this Sinful-Bodice Yoga quickly causes the destruction of the wife. 43.

### The Tearer Yoga (Variant)
If a malefic planet is in the fifth house and the Sun is in the seventh house from the lord of the house of death, this is also called the Tearer Yoga, which brings great suffering to the beloved. 44.

# Muhurta Kalpadruma: Vernacular Commentary
<page-num>85</page-num>

<margin>The Ocean of Joy Alignment</margin> When the planets are in their primary triangular positions <note>original: "Mulatrikona," a specific potent zodiacal position</note>, in their exalted houses, or in their own signs, this is the "Ocean of Joy" alignment. It is completely filled with the power to grant victory. 45. If the Sun, Saturn, and Moon are in the first <note>the Ascendant or "Tan"</note> and sixth <note>the house of enemies or "Ripu"</note> houses while in their best signs, they destroy all faults, just as a lion destroys a herd of deer. 46. If Mercury and Venus are both in the seventh house and the Moon is in the fourth house <note>the house of happiness or "Sukha-dham"</note>, enemies flee like villages of grass being blown away by a fierce wind. 47. If Venus is in the first house, Mercury in the second, and the Sun in the third house <note>the house of siblings/valor or "Sahaj-bhawan"</note>, a king who marches under this alignment grants his enemies the "gift of fear" <note>meaning he terrifies them</note>. 48. If Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> is in the first house, malefic planets are in the tenth <note>the house of action or "Karma"</note>, and the eleventh house <note>the house of gains or "Labha"</note> is occupied, the king who marches will put his enemies to flight on the battlefield. 49. If Saturn and Mars <note>original: "Bhu-nand"</note> are in the eleventh, sixth, or tenth houses, and Venus, Mercury, and Jupiter are strong, they give the punishment of sorrow to the enemies. 50.

->|| Narendra Meter ||<-

If Venus <note>the son of Bhrigu</note> comes into the eleventh or third house (the house of friends), and Jupiter <note>the teacher of the gods</note> casts his sight <note>aspect</note> from a cardinal house <note>Kendra</note>, and if the seventh and eighth houses are free from malefic planets, the king may attack his enemies. The tears from the eyes of those enemies' wives will extinguish the fire of their husband's wrath. 51. If Jupiter, the Sun, Mars, or Saturn are in their own houses or in their exalted signs, and if the Moon is also in its own house in the first house—so say the sages—a king marching in such an alignment will see the destruction of his enemies, just as great lords are broken by internal divisions in their lineage. 52. If the Moon is in the seventh, Jupiter in the first, Venus and Mercury in the tenth, and the Sun, Saturn, and Mars are situated in the eleventh and third houses; all the greatest enemies of a king who marches under this alignment will come under his control, just as servants remain under the control of hope while serving a master for a price. 53.

->|| Doha Meter ||<-

If Jupiter is in the first house in its exalted sign and the Moon is in the house of gains (the eleventh), the king marches to victory over his enemies, just as Lord Shiva <note>original: "Girish"</note> destroyed the demon Tripura. 54. If Venus is in a cardinal or angular house <note>Kendra or Kona</note> and the Sun is in the first house with the Moon, he who marches will conquer his enemy as a powerful lion conquers a deer. 55.

->|| Narendra Meter ||<-

If a king sets out under such a combination of stars, his enemies will fall into internal strife and be destroyed in an instant. 56. On this day, Lord Rama <insert>Raghunandan</insert> began his march without considering any other astrological charts; he was so powerful that his strength had no end, and he slew the ten-headed Ravana <insert>Dashakandh</insert>. 59. ||

### Now, regarding the twitching of limbs and omens at the time of travel ||

If the auspicious limbs (as previously described) twitch and the omens are favorable, and if the planetary alignment <term>Lagna</term> is strong and the mind is pure, then a king should depart for foreign lands. Even if the Lagna is not strong, but the omens are excellent, he may go. However, if the omens are bad and the mind is not pure, he should not go abroad. 60. || **Paddhari Meter** || During the thread ceremony, the installation of a deity, or a marriage, or during periods of birth/death impurity <term>Sutaka</term> or the Holi festival—if there is lightning, thunder, or snowfall, a king should not travel for seven days. 61. If a person goes to a city and returns to his own home within a single day after completing his work, then the lunar date, the day of the week, and the position of the Yogini <note>a directional energy</note> need not be considered; this is the secret opinion of the Sages. 62. ||

### Now, the consideration of Entry and Exit ||

**Narendra Meter** || On whatever date, day, and constellation <term>Nakshatra</term> a king enters a place, the ninth day from that is not auspicious for his exit. Similarly, on whatever date, day, and constellation he exits, if he enters a place on the ninth day from that, the lord of the land will suffer physical pain. 63. ||

# The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Times
<note>original: "Muhurtakalpadruma Bhasha"</note>

Worshiping and honoring all the saints who are supremely merciful; worshiping the elephants and horses, and taking blessings from the elders <note>Guru-jan</note>, the Great Commander of the army gives and receives honors. Listening to the songs of the women and making the sound of the kettle-drums, the King sets forth while giving charity to the Brahmins. 64.

**Now, the Dietary Requirements for the Lunar Mansions** <note>Nakshatra-dohada: specific foods to be eaten when traveling under certain stars to ensure success</note>.
Horse-gram, sesame sweets, and black gram; yogurt, clarified butter <note>ghee</note>, milk, and the meat of a deer. Deer blood is mentioned, and then milk-rice pudding <note>payasa</note>; the meat of a blue jay <note>chasha</note> should be ordered. 65.
The meat of a deer and that of a rabbit are mentioned; then the great poets consider the red *santhi* rice. Then *priyangu* millet and fried cakes <note>pupa</note> are mentioned as food; then the meat of a spotted bird and the eating of mangoes. 66.
The meat of a tortoise and that of a starling; the meat of a monitor lizard <note>godha</note> as well. The meat of a porcupine, sacrificial grains <note>havishya</note>, and *khichdi* <note>a dish of rice and lentils</note>; mung bean with rice, barley with rice, and fish. 67.
Variegated <note>spiced or colored</note> rice is mentioned next; the wise poets have kept this sequence correctly. These are the foods for the stars starting from Ashvini; the Kings eat all these when embarking on a journey. 68.

---

**Couplet** <note>Doha</note>:
Eat with pleasure those items which are called "edible" <note>bhakshya</note>; but only look upon <note>do not eat</note> those which are called "inedible" <note>abhakshya</note>. 69.

**Now, the Dietary Requirements for the Directions:**
Eat clarified butter for the East; sesame and rice for the South. For the West, fish; and for the North, the King should eat milk-rice pudding before departing. 70.

**Now, the Dietary Requirements for the Lunar Days:**
**Quatrain** <note>Chaupai</note>:
Sun-leaves <note>Calotropis</note> and four grains of rice; the Sage-King has determined this with certainty...

<page-num>88</page-num>
<header>The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Times (Muhurta-Kalpadruma).</header>

Drink water in which gold has been washed, or eat <term>Havishya</term><note>Food fit for an offering, usually simple and pure like rice and ghee</note>. Everyone, including the beloved and the learned, should follow this. 71
Drink the juice of the citron fruit, or water, then cow's urine, barley, <term>Payasa</term><note>A sweet milk and rice pudding</note>, and contemplate jaggery.
Blood <note>likely referring to red-colored juices or specific herbal preparations</note>, moong beans, and cooked rice, O brother; these are for the King who departs on the first lunar day and those following. 72

## Couplet
Eat <term>Shikharini</term><note>A spiced yogurt drink, similar to Shrikhand</note>, rice pudding, fermented gruel, cooked food, milk, and curd. Eat raw milk and sesame.

One should go for the sake of wealth and other gains after eating rice. 73 First, walk thirty-six steps starting with the right foot. Then, mount an elephant or other transport after giving gifts to all. 74 Fill a copper vessel with sesame and ghee, along with gold. To the East, go by horse; to the West, by chariot; to the South, the King, the crown of men, goes in a palanquin; and to the North, in a litter. 76
<margin>Regarding the Place of Departure for the King.</margin>
The King makes his departure from the house of his spiritual preceptor or from his own sleeping chambers. Or, as the wise poets describe, from the house of his most beloved queen. Then, honoring the words of the <term>Brahmins</term><note>The priestly class</note> and seeing auspicious omens with a joyful heart, that King attains great fame and much wealth through his policy and strength. 77 If there is a delay in the work, then perform the "symbolic departure" <note>Prasthana</note>. Place the Brahmin’s sacred thread, the King’s weapon, or a sweet fruit in a vessel. Sending a priest ahead with a coconut, place these items in a pure location; thus, one receives the fruit of the journey ahead. 78 Leaving one's own house to stay in another's house for this purpose was spoken by the wise Sage Garga.

# The Tree of Auspicious Timing: A Vernacular Commentary
<page-num>86</page-num>
<header>The Tree of Auspicious Timing: Vernacular Commentary</header>
<sig>52</sig>

Place the initial step as far as the eye can see; this was the instruction given by the sage Bharadwaj <note>A legendary Vedic sage credited with many architectural and military treatises</note>. However, the sage Vashistha says that one should establish their first camp outside the city limits to achieve great fame. 79.

### *Chaupai Verse*
Some say the first stop should be fifty bows <note>A "bow" (dhanush) is a traditional unit of length, approximately 6 feet</note> away from one's own temple or palace. Another sage declares it should be two hundred bows away, while some desire it to be only ten bows distant. 80. A bow is recognized by the wise to be four cubits <note>A cubit is the length from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger</note> in measure. In whichever direction victory is sought, one should make their initial departure <term>Prasthan: a ritualized first step or short journey taken at an auspicious moment before the actual long trip</term> in that direction. 81.

### *Narendra Verse*
For kings, the stay at the initial departure camp is said to be ten days. For the feudal lords and commanders, it is described as seven days. For the common people, the wise poets say it should be five days. Knowing the auspicious day, the virtuous man should bathe in the morning and then begin his journey. 82. For seven days prior to the day of departure, one should abstain from sexual intercourse. If the king is particularly lustful, he must abstain for at least one day. One should avoid drinking milk for three days and stay at the city gate for five days. If you desire your own welfare, abandon oil and sweets on the day of travel. 83.

### *Doha Verse*
Those kings who consume oil, meat, jaggery, or salt at the time of departure return to their homes suffering from disease. 84. Those who insult a Brahmin or their own wife while departing will wander from country to country and ultimately meet their end in misery. 85.

### *Narendra Verse*
The four months beginning with Pausha <note>Mid-December to mid-January; the four months are Pausha, Magha, Phalguna, and Chaitra</note>—if it rains during these, it is called "untimely rain" <term>Akala-vrishti</term> by everyone, and traveling during this time increases sorrow. If the ground is marked by the footprints of men or animals <note>indicating mud or soft earth from heavy rain</note>, one should not travel. If there are only a few drops, one may go; listen to what the good poets say. 86.

### *Doha Verse*
A little rain causes little harm; the wise explain it thus. But in heavy rain, there are many faults; do not make your departure then. 87. If the clouds thunder or rain falls just as the time for departure arrives, one should summon a Brahmin and donate a gold image of the Sun or Moon. 88. If an ill omen should meet one's sight during the departure, one should give a gift of clarified butter <note>Ghee</note> and gold, and then...

# The Tree of Auspicious Moments: Vernacular Commentary
<page-num>60</page-num>
->**On Omens**<-

**Dhanakshari Meter**
If you see a noble priest <note>original: "Vipravar"</note>, a horse, an elephant, fruit, grain, milk, a cow, mustard seeds, or gold; if you behold a courtesan, a drum, a treasury, fine silk, a blue jay <note>original: "Chash"</note>, a tethered animal, meat, or a fresh ritual vow; if you hear pleasant words or see a beautiful youth, a water-pot filled to the brim, a parasol, earth, jewels, or a young maiden; if you see a white head-covering, a bull, or a steady flame—write these down as auspicious omens for one's journey. (60)

If a beautiful woman with her son, or someone carrying a mirror and collyrium <note>eye-black</note> approaches; if a washerman <note>original: "Rajak"</note> meets you from the front carrying washed clothes; or if one sees fish or clarified butter <note>ghee</note>, they shall find happiness ahead. If there is no weeping behind you, and you see a throne, honey, a bow, a flag, or bright yellow pigment <note>original: "Rochan"</note>; or if you see a palanquin, hear the sound of the Vedas <note>scriptures</note>—poets sing that these omens bring success to any movement. (61)

**Couplet**
If someone meets you from behind carrying a water-pot that is not empty, know this to be a great omen, as the assembly of wise poets declares. (62)

**On Auspicious Omens Based on the Ascendant Lord**

**Couplet**
If a king begins his journey when Jupiter or Venus is in the Ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note>, and he meets three priests on the path, it is a very great omen. (63)

If Mercury or Venus are in the "Kanthaka" houses <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> and one meets a cow with her calf, know this also to be an auspicious omen, says the assembly of poets. (64)

If the Sun and Moon are in the tenth house, and one encounters flowers, a lamp, a mirror, or a washerman with clothes, the king will surely achieve his goal. (65)

If Mercury is in the fifth house and a sturdy bull comes from the front, or if Jupiter and the Moon are in the third house and a dog is seen on the left side, one obtains happiness. (66)

If all the planets fall in the ninth, tenth, or eleventh houses, and a mongoose, a blue jay, or a skylark <note>original: "Bharadwaj"</note> appears on the left side; (67)

If the Sun, Saturn, or Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> are in the third house, and one meets a fortunate married woman, a mature woman, or a young maiden on the path with affection; (68)

If Mars <note>the son of the Earth</note> is in the eleventh, tenth, sixth, or third house, and one meets wine or a beautiful woman, the journey proceeds with joy. (69)

If Jupiter and Mercury are in the seventh, eighth, or fifth...

### || Now, Regarding Inauspicious Omens ||

<term>Dandak</term> <note>A specific poetic meter characterized by long stanzas</note> || Do not look upon a barren woman, a person dressed in skins, a snake, bones, salt, fuel, charcoal, oil, or medicine. Do not look upon one smeared with fat, a drunkard, an enemy, a sick person, an ascetic with matted hair, grass, a sanyasi, a naked person, or one tormented by hunger. One who has applied oil but has not bathed, a person with a physical deformity of the nose, jaggery, blood, or a person with a skin disease—do not observe these with your mind. A menstruating woman, a chameleon, or a battle between cats; consider all these as inauspicious omens at the time of departure. 2. One whose clothes are stained with mud, or who quarrels within their own family, or a wicked person—be fearful of these. A widow, black grain, a buffalo, a person in a rage of battle, a person with a shaven head, or a pregnant woman—these should not meet your sight. Do not let the words of the blind, the deaf, or harsh speech, or the sight of wet clothes, or the <term>Khara-rava</term> <note>The braying of a donkey</note> reach your right ear. Seeing a monitor lizard, a hog, a rabbit, or a snake is considered auspicious, but hearing their cry is inauspicious and should be avoided. 3.

<term>Doha</term> <note>A rhyming couplet</note> || While crossing a river, in fear, in battle, or when an object is lost—in these specific situations, an inauspicious omen is actually good, and a "good" omen is of no benefit. 4. The cuckoo, the house lizard, the weaver bird, the <term>Ratla</term> <note>A specific species of bird or small animal</note>, the jackal, and the shrew—if these appear, or if the <term>Pingala</term> <note>A species of small owl</note> is on the left side, they are always said to be givers of happiness. 5. The hawk, the <term>Sacha</term>, the <term>Khamrig</term>, the <term>Chatak</term> <note>The Jacobin Cuckoo, legendary for drinking only raindrops</note>, and the monkey—these, along with the crow and the blue jay on the right side, are always givers of happiness. 6. The <term>Vojharin</term> bird on the right is always a giver of happiness. The <term>Khara-rava</term> <note>The braying of a donkey</note> on the left side is also known to be a good sign for those who understand. 7. If a first inauspicious omen occurs, one should perform eleven <term>Pranayama</term> <note>Regulated yogic breathing exercises</note> cycles. By doing this and then proceeding, the soul attains immense peace. 8. If a second omen occurs, perform sixteen cycles; if a third occurs, the journey should not take place. Knowing the opinion of the scriptures, this is spoken by the lineages of the poets. 9.

...that woman is destroyed. Regarding living in such a house, it is said that in the fire <note>referring to the Agni-kon or South-East direction</note>, the beloved sons and wealth are ruined. ### Now, the Description of Flaws at the Time of Travel <note>original: "Yatra-samaye-dosha-varnanam"</note> || Meter: Narendrashri ||

The Sages speak of the flaws regarding the stars <note>Nakshatras</note>, months, lunar days <note>Tithis</note>, and the direction of the time-spirit <note>Kaal-vaas</note>. Furthermore, the "Spike of the Day" <note>Bar-shool; a direction forbidden on specific weekdays</note>, the facing of the Yogini <note>a mystical energy</note>, and the "Limit-Knowledge" <note>Mit-gyan</note> are considered. The retrograde motion of Venus, the "Iron Bar" <note>Parigha</note> and "Staff" <note>Danda</note> yogas, and the inauspicious flaws of women are noted. One must also avoid the period of birth-impurity <note>Sutak</note>, the fortnight of death-mourning, the "Empty" <note>Rikta</note> lunar days, the sixth lunar day, and the "Anger of the Sun." The flaws of the Earth, the Moon being behind or to the left, and the "Obstacle" <note>Adal</note> are mentioned. Know the direction of Yama <note>South</note>, the five-fold flaw <note>Panchak</note>, and the Abhijit star; the King who ignores these descriptions faces many troubles. 12.

The eighth sign from one's birth sign or birth ascendant should not be the rising sign <note>Lagna</note> at the time of departure. The sign of the enemy (the sixth house) or its lord should not be the rising sign. Avoid the signs of Aquarius and Pisces. One should fear the "Thorn" <note>Kantak</note> and "Reverse" <note>Prateep</note> planetary flaws, and the day of one's own marriage. The wise describe these for kings and men; through the calculation of these, one's glory remains intact. 13.

->Thus ends the Chapter on Travel in the "Tree of Desired Times" composed by Shri Shambhunath.<-

### Now, the Commencement of a House <note>original: "Griharambha"</note> || Meter: Dandaka ||

If the city's zodiac sign falls in the ninth, tenth, eleventh, or second house from your own birth sign, and you reside there, you will never be sad. Instead, you will obtain sons, grandsons, and various types of wealth. Furthermore, take your own phonetic group <note>Varga; a classification of the alphabet used in name-astrology</note>, multiply it by two, and combine it with the city's group. Divide the total by eight; if the remaining number is high, it gives happiness and wealth—so sing all the poets. 1.

### Now, the Rule for Doors <note>original: "Dvaravidhi"</note> || Meter: Chaupai ||

When the Moon is in Cancer, Pisces, or Scorpio, place the door facing East; so say the generous. 2.
In Virgo, Capricorn, or Taurus, place it in the South. In Gemini, Libra, or Aquarius, place it in the West. 3.
In Aries, Leo, or Sagittarius, place it in the North; the good poets say this brings all happiness. If the city's sign is Taurus, Leo, Capricorn, or Gemini, the resident becomes a dweller in the center of the city. 4.

The Bull <note>Vrusha</note> is said by the sages to face only the direction of the Gods <note>the North-East or North</note>. ### Regarding the Months and House Doors
**Verse (Chaupai):**
When the Sun is in Aquarius during the month of Phalguna <note>February–March</note>, the house entrance is said to face the East. When the Sun enters Leo or Cancer during Shravana <note>July–August</note>, the entrance should be made facing East or West. 23. In the month of Pausha <note>December–January</note> when the Sun is in Capricorn, everyone says the entrance should face East. When you hear that the Sun is in Aries or Taurus during the month of Vaishakha <note>April–May</note>, consider the doors to be North or South. 24. In the month of Margashirsha <note>November–December</note> when the Sun is in Libra or Scorpio, the poets say the entrance faces North or South. Under the constellations of Rohini, Mrigashira, Shatabhisha, Swati, Dhanishta, and Hasta, 25. along with Pushya, a house brings happiness. If one wishes to build a lying-in chamber <note>a room for childbirth</note>, it is said to be done specifically in the Punarvasu constellation. When Abhijit and Shravana constellations arrive, everyone sings of entering the home. 26. When the Sun is in Aries during the month of Chaitra...

...so some have spoken. If the Sun is in Taurus, it falls in the month of Jyeshtha <note>May-June</note>. In Cancer, it is sung as the month of Ashadha <note>June-July</note>. 27. In the sign of Leo, it is the month of Bhadrapada <note>August-September</note>. In Libra, it is described as Kartika <note>October-November</note>. In Scorpio, it is explained as Margashirsha <note>November-December</note>. In Capricorn, know it in your heart as the month of Pausha <note>December-January</note>. 28.

**Couplet**
Build the temple <note>or house</note> in the month of Magha <note>January-February</note> when the Sun is in Capricorn or Aquarius. Having observed their own traditions, various scholars describe it thus. 29.

**Shripati <note>Referring to the authority of the astronomer Shripati</note>**
Sorrow, grain, death, and wealth are the results; loss of money and war are said to occur. Death, destruction, wealth, and Lakshmi <note>Prosperity</note> are considered; fire and fortune are what the sages speak of. 30.

**Couplet**
Starting from the month of Chaitra <note>March-April</note>, know the fruits of these months. This opinion is considered superior, as stated by the virtuous poets. 31.
