# The Direction of the House Entrance According to Lunar Date and Phase

From the first to the eighth day of the dark fortnight <note>term: Krishna Paksha; the waning moon</note>, the East-facing door brings ruin. From the ninth to the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, do not place the door facing North. 32. From the new moon to the eighth day of the bright fortnight <note>term: Shukla Paksha; the waxing moon</note>, the West-facing door is considered unfavorable. Then, from the ninth to the fourteenth day of the bright fortnight, the saints forbid a South-facing entrance. 33.

### The Bull Diagram (Vrishachakra)
<note>The Vrishachakra is a symbolic diagram used in Vedic astrology to determine the auspiciousness of a construction site based on the position of the Sun and Moon.</note>

**Dandaka Meter:** Three stars on the head cause destruction by fire; four on the forelegs cause restlessness or displacement. Four on the hind legs provide stability; three on the back bring wealth and prosperity. Four on the right side bring financial gain. Three on the tail cause the destruction of the owner; four on the left side lead to wandering or theft. Three on the face cause physical pain and suffering. Counting from the Sun's constellation to the constellation of the day, one should consider these effects for the temple or house.

### The Bull Diagram (Vrishachakra)

| 3 | 4 | 4 | 3 | 4 | 3 | 4 | 3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fire | Fear | Stability | Wealth | Riches | Owner's Ruin | Wandering | Pain |

**Chaupai Meter:** Count from the Sun's constellation to the Moon's constellation. Seven are inauspicious, eleven are auspicious, and then ten are inauspicious—avoid these. This is how the day for beginning house construction is determined.

**Narendra Meter:** If the Sun, Moon, Mercury, or Venus are weak, set <note>invisible in the sky</note>, or in their debilitated signs, then the master of the house, his wife, his son, and his wealth will be destroyed respectively. If the Moon's constellation falls behind the house <note>relative to the entrance</note>, it causes displacement; if it is in front, it brings night-time harassment by thieves.

**Doha Meter:** If the constellation of the owner and the constellation of the house are the same, then all great poets say it leads to the destruction of the master of the house.

**Regarding the Constellations of the Directions:** Starting from Krittika and including Abhijit, the lords of poets say that seven stars reside in each of the directions starting from the East.

**Narendra Meter:** Do not build a house on the *Rikta* lunar days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, the New Moon, or the first day of the fortnight. Avoid the *Panchaka* period <note>a specific five-day astrological window</note>, Mercury's influence, and movable signs; also avoid Sundays and Tuesdays. Aside from the houses of death and loss, consider others beneficial. Malefic planets are considered auspicious if placed in the sixth or eleventh houses <note>the houses of enemies and gains</note>.

**Knowing the Mouth of Rahu:**
**Chaupai Meter:** When the Sun is in Pisces, Aries, or Taurus... <note>The text cuts off here, describing the movement of the shadow planet Rahu through the zodiac signs.</note>

Then, the mouth of Rahu <note>The "ascending node" of the moon, considered a shadow planet in Vedic astrology whose "mouth" or direction must be avoided</note> resides in the direction of Shiva <note>Northeast</note>. In Gemini, Cancer, and Leo, it is said to be in the Northwest. In Virgo, Libra, and Scorpio, it desires the Southwest. 40. In Sagittarius, Capricorn, and Aquarius, it stays in the Southeast. The whole world says this is the location of Rahu's face. On the day of an eclipse or when performing a ritual, one should keep Rahu's face to one's back or to the left. 41.

Consider the "House of Gods," the "House of Men," and the "House of Water." These are determined by the zodiac signs. Each of these three categories occupies three signs. They move in reverse through the intermediate directions. 42.

**Couplet**
For the construction of a temple (House of Gods), a residence (House of Men), or a reservoir (House of Water), keeping the face of Rahu to one's back brings all forms of happiness and success. 43.

### Regarding the Construction of Wells
A well should be constructed in the East, West, North, or Northeast. By doing so in these directions of the temple or house, the owner attains all the wealth of a king. 45.

...the home; by doing so, all the heart's desires are fulfilled. In the **South-East** direction, one should place the **kitchen** <note>original: "rasoi"</note>. In the **South** direction, one should remain for **sleeping** 46. In the **South-West** direction, the **armory** <note>original: "hathyār"</note> should be kept. In the **West** direction, one should sit and **eat**. In the **North-West** direction, one should desire to keep the **grain** 47. In the **North** direction, keep the **treasury**. In the **North-East** <note>original: "Dev disha" - the direction of the gods</note>, establish the **shrine**; by worshipping there and bowing, sorrows are removed. Between the **East** and the **South-East**, the poets say the **buttermilk house** <note>a room for churning and storing dairy</note> should always be located 48. Between the **South-East** and the **South**, place the **ghee** <note>clarified butter</note> jars with their mouths sealed. Between the **South** and the **South-West**, O King, know that the **latrine** <note>original: "setkhāno"</note> should be placed 49. In the space between the **South-West** and the **West** <note>original: "Varuna"</note>, there establish the **school** for studying. Between the **West** and the **North-West**, all the great sages say is the place for **weeping** <note>a private room for mourning or expressing grief</note> 50. That which is between the **North-West** and the **North** is the room suitable for **sexual union**. That which is between the **North** and the **North-East** is the place suitable for storing **medicines** 51. In the interval between the **North-East** and the **East** <note>original: "Purandara"</note>, the King keeps all his **sundry goods**. These **sixteen rooms** <note>original: "shodash geha"</note> have been described by the poets, which even the sages desire to hear 52.

### ## Regarding Immediate Astrological Considerations

If **Jupiter** is in the first house, the **Sun** is in the sixth <note>the house of enemies</note>, **Mercury** is in the seventh, **Venus** is in the fourth, and **Saturn** is in the third when the house is commenced, then it shall last for a **hundred years** 53. If **Venus** and the **Sun** are in the first house, **Mars** is in the third, and **Jupiter** is in the fifth, then that house lasts for **two hundred years**; O King, do not say otherwise 54. If **Venus** and **Mercury** are in the first house, the **Sun** is in the tenth or eleventh, and **Jupiter** is in a quadrant <note>1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th house</note>, the good poets sing that the house lasts for a **hundred years**. If **Jupiter** is in the fourth, the **Moon** in the tenth, and **Mars** and **Saturn** are in the eleventh, then that house remains for **eighty years**, filled with eternal joy 54.

In a bright fortnight <note>waxing moon</note>, if the Ascendant is **Pisces** with **Jupiter** in **Cancer**, or if the Ascendant is **Libra** with **Saturn** situated there, so says the poet Chayani: in such a house, the master gains the happiness of much penance. The Goddess **Lakshmi** <note>Goddess of wealth</note> resides there, and the whole world sings its praises 55. If in the dwelling...

# Muhurta Kalpadruma: Vernacular Commentary
<page-num>100</page-num>

If the tenth or seventh house is occupied <note>original: "dasamsatayai"</note>, the house will fall into the hands of others; it will not remain with the owner but pass to another's lineage. Understanding this principle well, the great poets have spoken of these flaws. 56. The constellations Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada, Rohini <note>collectively called the "Dhruva" or fixed stars</note>, Mrigashira, Pushya, Shravana, Shatabhisha <note>original: "jalpati"</note>, and Ashlesha <note>original: "ahi"</note>—in these, one should seek the guidance of Jupiter <note>Guru</note>. If the house is built on Jupiter's day (Thursday) in these stars, the result is the birth of a royal son. 57. Dhanishta <note>original: "vasu"</note>, Ashwini, Shatabhisha <note>original: "varuna"</note>, Vishakha, and Chitra <note>original: "taksha"</note> are associated with Venus <note>Bhrigu</note>. Know that if the house is built on Venus's day (Friday), it is said the temple <note>here meaning the home</note> will remain full of grain and wealth. 57. Magha, Revati, Mula, Purva Ashadha, and Pushya—if the construction occurs in these under the influence of Mars <note>Bhauma</note> on Mars's day (Tuesday), a person will suffer the pain of losing a son. Even if the work is completed, the great poets say the nest <note>the home</note> will be consumed by fire. 58.

## Section on Entering the Home

<term>Narendra</term> <note>A poetic meter consisting of 28 matras</note> || One should enter the home in the months of Madhava <note>the month of Vaishakha, April-May</note>, Jyeshtha <note>May-June</note>, or Magha <note>January-February</note>. Listen well: do not forget to avoid Sundays and Tuesdays. Choose the Fixed <note>Dhruva</note> or Soft <note>Mridu</note> constellations <note>Nakshatras</note>. Take the Ascendant <note>Lagna</note> in two ways—either Fixed or Dual-natured—and avoid inauspicious lunar days. Perform many fire sacrifices and charities in all eight directions of the gods. 1. <term>Doha</term> <note>A rhyming couplet</note> || If the Ascendant of the entry or the Moon sign falls in the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th house <note>Upachaya houses, signifying growth</note> from the birth sign, the King of Poets says one should then manifest the entry into the new house. 2. <term>Narendra</term> || If a house has been burnt by fire, submerged in water, broken out of fear of a King, or if it is an old temple or a house long abandoned by its owner—in such cases, one may enter during the months of Shravana <note>July-August</note>, Kartika <note>October-November</note>, or Margashirsha <note>November-December</note>. By consulting a learned Brahmin to find a good day, one obtains many joys. 3. <term>Doha</term> || When the constellations are Pushya, Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, or Swati, then one should perform the entry; at these times, the planetary combustion <note>Asta</note> is not considered a flaw. 4. Finding the Root <note>Mula</note>, Soft, Fixed, Swift, or Mobile constellations, one should worship the house and offer sacrifices to the spirits <note>Bhuta-bali</note> to obtain happiness. 5. <term>Narendra</term> || Place the benefic planets in the 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th, 5th, 9th, 11th, or 2nd houses. Place the malefic planets in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses. The 4th and 8th houses from the birth sign must be pure; one should never enter a house when the transit is in the 8th house from the birth sign, as it does not bring beauty or prosperity. 6. <term>Doha</term> || Taking a Brahmin, a full water-pot, and friends along, keep Venus to your back and the Sun to your left. 7. Those who enter a new home according to this method obtain happiness and wealth; so describes the King of Poets. 8. <term>Chaupai</term> <note>A four-line poetic meter</note> || If the door faces the East, I shall now give the consideration for that. Whatever Ascendant is found for the auspicious moment, count to the eighth from it. 9. From there, count five places to find the Sun; know that to be the "Left Sun" <note>an astrological positioning of the sun relative to the door</note>. For a South-facing door, count from the fifth...

# The Language of the Tree of Auspicious Times

Understand this. For a house facing the West, the second <note>referring to the zodiac sign or position</note> is described. For a house facing the North, it should be known from the eleventh position. In this manner, the **Left-Hand Sun** <note>original: "Vama-Ravi"</note> is explained; it is truly a source of great happiness for mankind. 11.

**Couplet**
For an East-facing house, take the Full <note>Purna</note> and Joyous <note>Nanda</note> dates; for the South, take the Prosperous <note>Bhadra</note> dates. For the directions of Varuna (West) and Kubera (North), declare the Victorious <note>Jaya</note> and Prosperous <note>Bhadra</note> dates respectively. 12.

**Poetic Meter**
For an East-facing house, take the ninth position; calculate immediately from the eighth Ascendant <term>Lagna: the specific zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a given time</term>. When the Sun <note>original: "Dinakar," the day-maker</note> is within five signs from that point, it is called the "Left-Hand Sun" by the assembly of poets. From the fifth sign for the South, from the second for the West, and from the eleventh for the North, the house-owner should calculate. By finding the dates of Fullness, Joy, Prosperity, and Victory in the East and other directions, a great king obtains manifold happiness. 13.

Place one star <term>Nakshatra: lunar mansion or star cluster</term> at the mouth of the Pot <note>original: "Kalasha"</note>; if placed there, the house burns with the flame of fire. Consider four stars each and place them in the four directions; the resulting fruit is certain and does not waver. If placed in the belly, there is a gain of wealth, but if placed in the middle of the "piercing" <note>original: "Vedha," a point of astrological affliction</note>, it quickly robs one of life. If three stars fall at the base, one receives much wealth; if three fall in the neck, the house attains stability. 14.

### The Tree of Desires for Auspicious Times: Vernacular Version.

<margin>Now, the procedure for entering a new home.</margin> **Ghanakshari Meter:** With a third canopy adorned with pearls, and with songs of celebration, let the ears drink in the nectar of music. Hearing the sound of Vedic chanting throughout the city, having worshipped the gods, one should receive blessings from the elders and the townspeople. To the astrologers, painters, and the many others gathered nearby, and to the beloved family priest, one should distribute gifts. With a smiling face, offering flowers and mounted upon an elephant, one should make the journey to enter the new residence. 15.

->Thus, by the command of the illustrious Prince Achal Singh, this was composed by Tripathi Shambhunath.<-
->The Chapter on Entering the Home in the "Tree of Desires for Auspicious Times" is concluded.<-

->The end. May there be auspiciousness!<-

---

->As the original manuscript was found, so has it been transcribed.<-
->If there be any errors or omissions, may friends and scholars correct them.<-

->A blessing in the name of Sri Radha-Krishna in Ghanakshari Meter:<-

As long as the Lord of Boars <note>The Varaha avatar of Vishnu who lifted the earth</note>, the Moon, the Tree of Paradise, and the Great Tortoise <note>Kurma, who supports the world</note> remain in their places; as long as the serpent Shesha remains as a dwelling; as long as gods, sages, humans, demons, and celestial musicians worship Ganesha first in their sacrifices; as long as Shambhu <note>Shiva</note> remains on the Snowy Mountain <note>Himalaya</note> with the Daughter of the Mountain <note>Parvati</note>; as long as the power of the Lord of Immortals <note>Indra</note> remains in the city of Amaravati; and as long as the lotuses bloom in the waters of Lake Manasarovar—so long may the rule of the royal scion Achalesh <note>Prince Achal Singh</note> endure! 1.

->Printed at the Press of Munshi Nawal Kishore (C.I.E.), Lucknow.<-
->February, Year 1862 A.D.<-

->Copyright is reserved by this Printing Press.<-

<summary>This concluding page describes the festive rituals for entering a new home and offers a poetic blessing for the longevity of the patron, Prince Achal Singh. It identifies the author as Tripathi Shambhunath and the publisher as the famous Nawal Kishore Press in 1862.</summary>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>This page serves as a preface or advertisement for a Hindi translation of the Manusmriti, published by the famous Nawal Kishore Press. It outlines the contents of the legal and expiatory sections of the text and introduces the translator, Pandit Durga Prasad Shukla.</meta>

The consideration of gifts, the consideration of buying and selling goods, the consideration of the duty of service, the consideration of the violation of secret royal agreements and time-signals, the consideration of disputes regarding wages, labor, and rent, the consideration of evil-doers such as gamblers, the consideration of verbal abuse and physical assault, the consideration of thieves, dacoits, and robbers, and a very detailed description of various crimes, wrongdoers, and various practices under royal patronage.

In the **"Section on Atonement,"** <note>original: "Prayashchitta-kanda"</note> there are descriptions of the methods of offering water, the duration of impurity following a birth or death <note>original: "ashaucha-sutaka"</note>, the rules for immediate purification, the detailed expansion of the creation of the universe, the combination of intellect and other elements, the faults requiring atonement, the names and nature of hells and similar realms, the different characteristics of great sins and lesser sins, the description of atonement for major sins such as the intentional drinking of wine, the atonement for the theft of gold, the description of atonement for the killing of the lowly, and the nature and rules of every matter are described. However, because this extensive book was in Sanskrit, it was not accessible to the general public. Therefore, for the benefit of the people of India, the head of the printing house, the honorable **Munshi Nawal Kishore**, provided many resources and rewards to **Pandit Durga Prasad Shukla**, a resident of Agra and a lover of tradition, to have it translated into simple, common language and printed in his own press. It is hoped that any tradition-loving person who views this will be pleased to accept it and will thank the head of the printing house.

## Advertisement for the Annotated Manusmriti

This book, the *Manusmriti*, is the leader of all scriptures on Law and Duty <note>original: "Dharmashastras"</note> and is worshiped by all lovers of righteousness. Its authority and dignity are well-established throughout the world. Although many translations of this book have been made in languages such as Braj and Yaminya <note>likely referring to Urdu or a Persianized style</note>, none of them are such that everyone can easily understand the resolution of every matter and know its true intent. Therefore, for the benefit of all those devoted to religious duties and those who delight in the juice of knowledge, and for those of small—

It emphasizes making the complex Sanskrit legal code accessible to the general public for their moral and social benefit.</summary>

--- Page 107 ---
<meta>This page serves as the formal introduction or preface to a vernacular commentary on the Manusmriti, acknowledging the patron and the scholar responsible for the work.</meta>

->For the assistance of the Society for the Advancement of the Language of the Fort,<-
The illustrious **Munshi Naval Kishore** (C.I.E.) <note>Companion of the Order of the Indian Empire; a prominent book publisher in 19th-century India</note>—who is the pinnacle of all righteous actions, devoted to the preservation of social order, abundant in merit, skilled among the virtuous, adorned with all opulence, untainted by flaws, of noble lineage, and a destroyer of wicked intentions—has expended a great deal of wealth to have this work prepared. He commissioned the great and venerable teacher, **Pandit Mihir Chand Ji**, a jewel among the assembly of the talented and a leader in the sacred law-books <term>Dharmashastra; ancient Indian treatises on law and social conduct</term>, to create a comprehensive explanation in the local language <note>Hindi/Hindustani</note>. 

This work, titled the **Manvartha Bhaskara** <note>The Sun of Manu's Meaning</note>, is enriched with the essence of other legal scriptures, blended with their core principles, and contains the hidden secrets of all previous commentaries. It features the word-for-word breakdown, prose order, intended meaning, and spiritual significance, along with the original verses. It has been printed and published at his own press located in Lucknow <note>original: "Lakshmanpur", the traditional name for Lucknow</note>.

Within this volume, all the duties and religious obligations of the world pertaining to the four classes <term>Varna; the social orders of Brahmin, Kshatriya, Vaishya, and Shudra</term> and the four stages of life <term>Ashrama; Student, Householder, Forest-dweller, and Renunciant</term> are described in great detail. 

Furthermore, it contains a complete account of the world, including:
*   The origin of the universe and the creation of the heavens and the earth.
*   The creation of the gods and the discernment between righteousness and unrighteousness.
*   The birth of Manu <note>the progenitor of humanity</note> and the origins of celestial beings like the Yakshas and Gandharvas.
*   The origins of clouds, animals, birds, worms, insects, and the four types of life: those born from the womb, from eggs, from sweat, and from the earth <note>seeds/sprouts</note>.
*   The origins of vegetation, shrubs, vines, and trees.
*   The measurements of day and night and the duration of the cosmic ages <term>Yugas</term>.
*   The rules and rewards for performing vows and fasts.
*   Descriptions of various lands and the essential rites for humans, such as birth ceremonies, naming ceremonies, the first haircut, and the initiation with the sacred thread.
*   The methods and rules for studying the Vedas and techniques for controlling the senses.
*   Descriptions of the roles of the Preceptor, the Sub-teacher, and the Guru.
*   The rules and prohibitions for ancestral rites <term>Shraddha</term>, as well as matters concerning atonement <term>Prayashchitta</term>.

All these subjects are described excellently and in detail. It is hoped that the learned scholars and gentlemen who cherish the sacred laws and social order will, upon viewing this, be greatly pleased and grant their blessings to the author and the director of the printing press. Should any errors have remained in the printing of such a vast volume, may they kindly forgive the offense.

<summary>This preface introduces the "Manvartha Bhaskara," a Hindi commentary on the Laws of Manu commissioned by the famous publisher Munshi Naval Kishore and authored by Pandit Mihir Chand. It outlines the encyclopedic scope of the text, covering everything from cosmic creation to social duties and rituals.</summary>
